\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(I):=\mathcal{L}_{r_{1}}(I):=\Phi_{I}\left(\mathcal{L}_{r_{1}}(\hat{I})\right)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{L}(S):=\mathcal{L}_{r_{2}}(S):=\Phi_{S}\left(\mathcal{L}_{r_{2}}(\hat{S})\right).

Clearly,
\dim\Pi^{r}(I)=\#\mathcal{L}_{r}(I)
and
\dim\Pi^{r}(S)=\#\mathcal{L}_{r}(S)
which yields
\dim\Pi_{t,\bm{x}}^{r_{1},r_{2}}(I\times S)=\#\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
for the
anisotropic Lagrangian nodes

\mathcal{L}(I\times S):=\mathcal{L}(I)\times\mathcal{L}(S)
. In order to recall the definition of these anisotropic polynomial spaces, see
Section

2.1.1
. In particular:

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a conforming simplicial triangulation of
\Omega
and
S,S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}
. Then

\displaystyle S\cap\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})=S^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}(S).

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of fact that
S\cap S^{\prime}
is a common hyperface of
S
and
S^{\prime}
, due to the conformity of
\mathcal{T}
, and the definition of the lagrange nodes.
∎

Throughout the rest of this section we consider a space-time partition
\mathcal{P}
derived by finitely many applications of
\textup{PATCH}\_\textup{REFINE}(\cdot,\cdot,d,s_{1},s_{2})
, for fixed
s_{1},s_{2}\in(0,\infty)
, starting with
\mathcal{P}_{0}
from
Section

3
. Thus,
\mathcal{P}
is a non-overlapping partition of
\Omega_{T}
which fulfills the properties
(i)
and
(ii)
from the beginning of
Section

3.2
. Recall that on
\mathcal{P}
the space of
continuous anisotropic finite elements
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}=\left\{F\in C(\Omega_{T},\mathbb{R})\mid F_{|I\times S}\in\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}(I\times S),\,I\times S\in\mathcal{P}\right\}.

The set of all local degrees of freedom is given by
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}):=\bigcup\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
. In particular, for the (possibly)
discontinuous
version of the above finite element space, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P},\textup{DC}}=\left\{F:\Omega_{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\mid F_{|I\times S}\in\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}(I\times S),\,I\times S\in\mathcal{P}\right\},

\dim\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P},\textup{DC}}=\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}}\#\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
holds true.

However, due to the continuity condition for the finite elements, the local degrees are not all global degrees of freedom. In the following theorem we will see that the set of
free nodes

\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P}):=\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P})\setminus\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})
indeed corresponds to the global degrees of freedom. Here,
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})
is the set of
hanging nodes
in
\mathcal{P}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P}):=\{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P})\mid\exists\,I\times S\in\mathcal{P}:\nu\in\overline{I\times S}\ \text{and}\ \nu\notin\mathcal{L}(I\times S)\}.

Theorem 4.2
.

For
\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
,
\phi_{\nu}\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
is well-defined by
\phi_{\nu}(\nu^{\prime})=\delta_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
for all
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
. Further,
(\phi_{\nu})_{\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})}
forms a basis of
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
.

Proof.

In
[
Grä11
, Thm. 3.1]
, the corresponding result has been shown for simplicial meshes covering a polyhedral Lipschitz domain and fulfilling the analogue to the
“hierarchical structure”
-property from
3.13
(i)
. Since our meshes also fulfill this property, one can obtain the above corresponding result following the lines of the proof (and its prerequisites) there.
∎

Illustrative example for
r_{1}=3
and
r_{2}=2

The main result of this section is the following characterization of
\supp\phi_{\nu}
, for
\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
, which will be essential for the construction of an appropriate interpolation operator.

Theorem 4.3
.

Let
\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
. If
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}
and
\nu\in\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
, then
\supp\phi_{\nu}\subset\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)
, with
j(1):=2
and
j(d):=3
for
d\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
. Furthermore,

|I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}|\sim_{d,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|\supp\phi_{\nu}|,

for all
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}
such that
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\subset\supp\phi_{\nu}
.

In particular, it follows from Theorem
4.3
that the support of
\phi_{\nu}
is contained in the extended neighborhood of degree 3 of the prism
I\times S
if
\dim(S)\geq 2
, and of degree 2 if
\dim(S)=1
. Moreover, the total measure of
\supp\phi_{\nu}
is comparable to the measure of any prism contained in the support. The rest of this section is dedicated to the proof of this theorem, which we develop through a series of rather technical lemmas.

First, we consider the set of elements of
\mathcal{P}
that cause
\nu\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})
to
“hang”
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu):=\{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}\mid\nu\in\overline{I\times S}\ \text{and}\ \nu\notin\mathcal{L}(I\times S)\}

We observe the following:

Remark 4.4
.

(i)

If
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
, clearly
\nu\in\partial(I\times S)=(\partial I\times S)\cup(\mathring{I}\times\partial S)
, since
\mathcal{P}
is a non-overlapping partition of
\Omega_{T}
. In particular, the above union representation is disjoint.

(ii)

Decomposing
\nu=(\nu_{t},\nu_{\bm{x}})
with
\nu_{t}\in[0,T]\subset\mathbb{R}
and
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
yields

\displaystyle\nu=(\nu_{t},\nu_{\bm{x}})\in\mathcal{L}(I\times S)\quad\iff\quad\nu_{t}\in\mathcal{L}(I)\text{ and }\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{L}(S).

These observations can be used to investigate the geometry of hanging nodes more precisely. First, the following lemmata will shed some light on the mesh geometry around hanging nodes, if the nodes
“hang in space”
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
\nu\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})\cap\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
for some
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}
and
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
with
\nu\in\partial I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}
. Then,

\displaystyle\nu\in(\partial I\times S)\cap(\partial I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{I}\cap\overline{I^{\prime}}=\partial I\cap\partial I^{\prime}=\{\nu_{t}\}

Proof.

Since
\nu_{t}\in\partial I^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime})
,
\nu_{\bm{x}}\notin\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})
follows due to
4.4
(ii)
and the assumption
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
. Therefore,
\nu_{t}\in\mathcal{L}(I)\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime})\subset\overline{I}\cap\overline{I^{\prime}}
, since
\nu\in\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
.
I
and
I^{\prime}
are both results of classical bisections of elements in
\mathcal{I}_{0}
, which is a disjoint partition of
[0,T]
. Therefore, either
\#(\overline{I}\cap\overline{I^{\prime}})=1
, which would already imply the assertion, or
|I\cap I^{\prime}|>0
. If the latter was true, there would be an
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}
(with
‘small’
modulus), such that
\nu_{t}+\varepsilon\in I\cap I^{\prime}
, i.e.,
S,S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}(\nu_{t}+\varepsilon,\mathcal{P})
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\nu_{\bm{x}}\in S^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}(S)=S\cap\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})\subset\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime}),

due to
4.1
and the conformity of
\mathcal{T}(\nu_{t}+\varepsilon,\mathcal{P})
, which is a contradiction to
\nu_{\bm{x}}\notin\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})
.
∎

This leads us directly to the following result.

Lemma 4.6
.

We stay in the setting of
4.5
. Then there exists
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
such that
\nu\in\partial I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}
,
S\in\textup{BISECT}(S^{\prime\prime},d)
, and the Lagrange nodes of
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}
in the interior of the facet
\{\nu_{t}\}\times S^{\prime\prime}
of
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}
are free, i.e.,

\displaystyle(\{\nu_{t}\}\times\mathring{S^{\prime\prime}})\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P}).

If
d=1
, also

\displaystyle(\{\nu_{t}\}\times\partial S^{\prime\prime})\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P}).

Illustration in the case of
r_{2}=4
, i.e., elements that are cubic in space

Proof.

From
4.5
, we obtain that
\nu\in(\partial I\times S)\cap(\partial I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})
and
\overline{I}\cap\overline{I^{\prime}}=\partial I\cap\partial I^{\prime}=\{\nu_{t}\}
. Without loss of generality we can therefore assume that
t<t^{\prime}
for all
(t,t^{\prime})\in I\times I^{\prime}
. Then
S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}(\nu_{t},\mathcal{P})
and setting
\varepsilon_{0}:=\min\limits_{J\times R\in\mathcal{P}}\textup{diam}(J)
, yields
S\in\mathcal{T}(\nu_{t}-\varepsilon,\mathcal{P})
for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
. Therefore, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{down}:=\mathcal{T}\left(\nu_{t}-\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{2},\mathcal{P}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{T}_{up}:=\mathcal{T}\left(\nu_{t},\mathcal{P}\right).

Now we consider the set
M
of elements of
\mathcal{T}_{up}
that overlap with
S
(and contain
\nu_{x}
), i.e.,

\displaystyle M:=\{S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{up}\mid|S\cap S^{\prime\prime}|>0\quad\text{and}\quad\nu_{\bm{x}}\in S^{\prime\prime}\}

Let us now choose an arbitrary
S^{\prime\prime}\in M
. Due to the construction of
\mathcal{T}_{up}
, there is a unique interval
I^{\prime\prime}
such that
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}
and
\nu\in\overline{I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}}
.

First, we will show that
I
and
I^{\prime\prime}
do not overlap. Due to
|S\cap S^{\prime\prime}|>0
,
|I\cap I^{\prime\prime}|>0
would imply that
|(I\times S)\cap(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})|>0
. Since
\mathcal{P}
is an non-overlapping covering of
\Omega_{T}
, this would yield
I\times S=I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}
, in particular,
S=S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{up}
. Therefore, since
S^{\prime},S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{up}
,
4.1
implies that

\displaystyle\nu_{\bm{x}}\in S^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}(S)=S^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime})=S^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})\subset\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime}),

(9)

due to the conformity of
\mathcal{T}_{up}
. Now this would be a contradiction to our assumption
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
, according to
4.4
(ii)
, because
\nu_{t}\in\partial I^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime})
.

Therefore, we know that
\overline{I}\cap\overline{I^{\prime\prime}}=\partial I\cap\partial I^{\prime\prime}=\{\nu_{t}\}
(which yields
\nu\in\partial I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}
, since
S^{\prime\prime}\in M
) and
\dim(S\cap S^{\prime\prime})=d
. So the
1
-irregular rule in time implies

\displaystyle|\ell(I\times S)-\ell(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})|\leq 1\quad\iff\quad|\ell(S)-\ell(S^{\prime\prime})|\leq 1.

Now we distinguish the three cases that this inequality allows for, regarding the relationship between
S
and
S^{\prime\prime}
. Proceeding as in (
9
), we obtain that
S=S^{\prime\prime}
is not possible, since it would again imply
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})
. If
S^{\prime\prime}\in\textup{BISECT}(S,d)
was true, this would yield
\mathcal{L}(S)\subset\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime})
, which would in turn lead to the same contradiction as for
S=S^{\prime\prime}
, if we replace the first
‘
=
’
in (
9
) by
‘
\subset
’
. Therefore,
S\in\textup{BISECT}(S^{\prime\prime},d)
is true.

Furthermore,
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
because
\nu_{\bm{x}}\notin\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime})
has to be true, as one can see as follows: If
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime})
was true, then
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})
, since
S^{\prime},S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{up}
, which is conforming. But this would be a contradiction to
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
as it has been used multiple times in the argumentation above.

The assertion(s) about the facet
\{\nu_{t}\}\times S^{\prime\prime}
of
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}
remain(s) to be shown. In fact, this really is a facet, since
\nu_{t}\in\partial I^{\prime\prime}
as shown above. Now assume
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})\cap(\{\nu_{t}\}\times\mathring{S^{\prime\prime}})
and denote
\nu^{\prime}=(\nu_{t},\nu^{\prime}_{\bm{x}})
with
\nu^{\prime}_{\bm{x}}\in\mathring{S^{\prime\prime}}\subset\Omega
. Since
S\in\textup{BISECT}(S^{\prime\prime},d)
,
\{R\in\mathcal{T}_{up}\cup\mathcal{T}_{down}\mid\nu^{\prime}_{\bm{x}}\in R\}=\{S,\tilde{S},S^{\prime\prime}\}
, with
\textup{BISECT}(S^{\prime\prime},d)=\{S,\tilde{S}\}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\{J\times R\in\mathcal{P}\mid\nu^{\prime}\in J\times R\}\subset\{I\times S,I\times\tilde{S},I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}\}.

Since,
\nu_{t}\in\partial I\cap\partial I^{\prime\prime}\subset\mathcal{L}(I)\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime})
and
\mathcal{L}(S),\mathcal{L}(\tilde{S})\supset\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime})
, we conclude that
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
.

Lastly, consider the case
d=1
and
\nu^{\prime}\in\{\nu_{t}\}\times\partial S^{\prime\prime}
. If
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})
, there would have to exist
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu^{\prime})
. Thus,
S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{up}\cup\mathcal{T}_{down}
with
\nu^{\prime}_{\bm{x}}\in R
. Then either, as before
S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\{S,\tilde{S},S^{\prime\prime}\}
and therefore
\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime})\subset\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime\prime})
, or
\#(S^{\prime\prime}\cap S^{\prime\prime\prime})=1
, which also yields
\nu^{\prime}_{\bm{x}}\in\partial S^{\prime\prime}\cap\partial S^{\prime\prime\prime}\subset\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime})\cap\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime\prime})
. So
\nu_{t}\notin\partial I^{\prime\prime\prime}
would have to hold in order to justify
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu^{\prime})
. But this in particular implies that
|I^{\prime\prime\prime}\cap I|>0
and
|I^{\prime\prime\prime}\cap I^{\prime\prime}|>0
, since
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}
then has to touch
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}
and
I\times S
or
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}
and
I\times\tilde{S}
. In turn, this yields
\ell(I^{\prime\prime\prime})<\ell(I^{\prime\prime})
, i.e.,
\ell(I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime})=\ell(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})-1=\ell(I\times S)-2
, where we have used the 1-irregular rule in space in the first step. However, the latter conclusion is a contradiction to this very rule, since
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
I\times S
touch in time. This ends the proof.
∎

Now, we will shed some further light regarding the nodes on the edges of the facet from
4.6
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\nu\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})\cap\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
and
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
be the element from
4.6
. Further, consider a node
\nu^{\prime}\in\{\nu_{t}\}\times\partial S^{\prime\prime}
. Then either,
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
or
\nu^{\prime}

“hangs in time”
, i.e., it exists
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu^{\prime})
with
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathring{I^{\prime\prime\prime}}\times\partial S^{\prime\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu^{\prime})=\emptyset
, then
\nu^{\prime}\notin\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})
and thus
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
. Otherwise, consider
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu^{\prime})
. In contrast to the assertion, assume
\nu^{\prime}\in\partial I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}
. Then,
4.5
yields
\partial I\cap\partial I^{\prime\prime}\cap\partial I^{\prime\prime\prime}=\{\nu_{t}\}
,
S\in\textup{BISECT}(S^{\prime\prime},d)
, and
S^{\prime\prime}\in\textup{BISECT}(S^{\prime\prime\prime},d)
without loss of generality. Further,
S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{down}
or
S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{up}
, where we use the terminology from the proof of
4.6
. In any case, this is a contradiction to the fact that
\mathcal{T}(t,\mathcal{P})
is a non-overlapping triangulation of
\Omega
for any
t\in[0,T]
(see
3.1
(ii)
), since
|S\cap S^{\prime\prime\prime}|,|S^{\prime\prime}\cap S^{\prime\prime\prime}|>0
,
S\in\mathcal{T}_{down}
, and
S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{up}
.
∎

Now we consider nodes which
“hang in time”
.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
\nu\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})\cap\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
for some
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}
and
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
with
\nu\in\mathring{I^{\prime}}\times\partial S^{\prime}
. Then,

\displaystyle\nu\in(\overline{I}\times\partial S)\cap(\mathring{I^{\prime}}\times\partial S^{\prime}),\quad 0\leq\dim(S\cap S^{\prime})\leq d-1,\quad\text{and}\quad\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})\cap(\partial S\cap\partial S^{\prime}).

Proof.

\nu_{t}\in\overline{I}\cap\mathring{I^{\prime}}
implies
|I\cap I^{\prime}|>0
. Due to
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in S\cap S^{\prime}
,
\dim(S\cap S^{\prime})\geq 0
. If
\dim(S\cap S^{\prime})=d
held,
|S\cap S^{\prime}|>0
would hold, too, which would imply
|(I\times S)\cap(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})|>0
. Since
\mathcal{P}
is a non-overlapping partition of
\Omega_{T}
this would imply that
I\times S=I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}
, in contradiction to
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
. Therefore, we obtain the asserted
\dim(S\cap S^{\prime})\in\{0,\dots,d-1\}
. This yields
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\partial S\cap\partial S^{\prime}
. Lastly, choose
t\in\mathring{I}\cap\mathring{I^{\prime}}
. Since
S,S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}(t,\mathcal{P})
,
S^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}(S)=S\cap\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})\subset\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})
due to
4.1
and the conformity of
\mathcal{T}(t,\mathcal{P})
. Since,
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in S^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}(S)
, this concludes the proof.
∎

Together,
4.5
and
4.8
show that a hanging node can either
“hang”
in time or in space, but not in both.

Corollary 4.9
.

Let
\nu\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})\cap\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
for some
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}
. Then either
\nu\in\partial I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}
for all
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
or
\nu\in\mathring{I^{\prime}}\times\partial S^{\prime}
for all
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
.

Proof.

Assume the existence of
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime},I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
with
\nu\in(\partial I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})\cap(\mathring{I^{\prime\prime}}\times\partial S^{\prime\prime})
. We can assume
\{\nu_{t}\}=\partial I\cap\partial I^{\prime}\cap\mathring{I^{\prime\prime}}
due to
4.5
and
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime})
due to
4.8
, correspondingly, without loss of generality. The first property implies that
|I^{\prime}\cap I^{\prime\prime}|>0
, thus one can choose
t\in\mathring{I^{\prime}}\cap\mathring{I^{\prime\prime}}
. Since
\mathcal{T}(t,\mathcal{P})
is conforming and
S^{\prime},S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}(t,\mathcal{P})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\nu_{\bm{x}}\in S^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime})=S^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})\subset\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})

due to
4.1
and to the second property above. This is a contradiction to
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
.
∎

Now we show the analogue to
4.6
for nodes that
“hang in time”
.

Lemma 4.10
.

We stay in the setting of
4.8
. Then there exists
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
such that
\nu\in\mathring{I^{\prime\prime}}\times\partial S^{\prime\prime}
,
I\in\textup{BISECT}(I^{\prime\prime},1)^{\otimes k}
, with
1\leq k\leq k_{0}=k_{0}(d,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and the Lagrange nodes of
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}
in the interior of the line segment
I^{\prime\prime}\times\{\nu_{\bm{x}}\}
of
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}
are free, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathring{I^{\prime\prime}}\times\{\nu_{\bm{x}}\}\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P}).

If
d=1
, also

\displaystyle\partial I^{\prime\prime}\times\{\nu_{\bm{x}}\}\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P}).

Illustration in the case of
r_{1}=3
, i.e., elements that are quadratic in time

Proof.

In the case of
d=1
, the temporal and spatial directions are interchangeable, and therefore the second assertion already follows from
4.10
.

Now let us consider the case of
d\geq 2
. Due to
4.9
, we know that
\nu_{t}\in\mathring{I^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime})
and
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})
for any
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
. In particular,
\nu_{t}\in\overline{I}\cap\mathring{I^{\prime}}
implies
|I\cap I^{\prime}|>0
. Therefore, either
I\subset I^{\prime}
or
I^{\prime}\subset I
due to the construction of
\mathcal{P}
. If the latter was true,
\nu_{t}\in\mathcal{L}(I)\cap\overline{I^{\prime}}\cap\overline{I}\subset\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime})
, which cannot hold, since
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\notin\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
and
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})
. Thus,
I\subsetneq I^{\prime}
and therefore
I\in\textup{BISECT}(I^{\prime},1)^{\otimes k(I^{\prime})}
for some
k(I^{\prime})\in\mathbb{N}
. This
k(I^{\prime})
is bounded by a constant
k_{0}=k_{0}(d,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}})\in\mathbb{N}
, since
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)
and

\displaystyle|\ell(I_{1}\times S_{1})-\ell(I_{2}\times S_{2})|\lesssim_{\,d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}}}1\quad\text{for any}\quad I_{1}\times S_{1},I_{2}\times S_{2}\in\omega_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S).

Now consider,
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
with
\ell(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})=\argmin\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)}\ell(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})
, then
k(I^{\prime})\leq k(I^{\prime\prime})
and
I^{\prime}\subset I^{\prime\prime}
. Now let
\nu^{\prime}:=(\nu^{\prime}_{t},\nu_{\bm{x}})\in(\mathring{I^{\prime\prime}}\times\{\nu_{\bm{x}}\})\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})
and assume that
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})
. Then there is
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu^{\prime})
. First, assume that
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathring{I^{\prime\prime\prime}}\times\partial S^{\prime\prime\prime}
for
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu^{\prime})
. Since
\nu_{t}^{\prime}\in\mathring{I^{\prime\prime}}\cap\mathring{I^{\prime\prime\prime}}
,
|I^{\prime\prime}\cap I^{\prime\prime\prime}|>0
. As before, we can therefore conclude that
I^{\prime\prime}\subsetneq I^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\ell(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})>\ell(I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime})
. But this would imply
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
, in contrast of the minimality condition used in the choice of
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}
. Second, consider the case
\nu^{\prime}\in\partial I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}
. According to
4.6
,
\nu_{t}^{\prime}\in\partial I^{\prime\prime}\cap\partial I^{\prime\prime\prime}
and therefore
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in(\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime}))\setminus\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime\prime})
. But
S^{\prime\prime},S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}(t,\mathcal{P})
for
t\in\mathring{I^{\prime\prime}}\cap\mathring{I^{\prime\prime\prime}}
, thus the conformity in space yields
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in S^{\prime\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime})=S^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime\prime})\subset\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime\prime})
due to
4.1
. But this is a contradiction to
\nu_{t}\in\partial I^{\prime\prime\prime}\subset\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime\prime})
and
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu^{\prime})
.
∎

In contrast to the edges on the boundary of the face in
4.6
and
4.7
, we can show that the edges of the line segment in
4.10
are indeed also free.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
\nu\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})\cap\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
and
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu)
be the element from
4.10
that is there called
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}
. Additionally, let a node
\nu^{\prime}\in\partial I^{\prime}\times\{\nu_{\bm{x}}\}
. Then,
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
.

Proof.

As before, we know that
I^{\prime}\supsetneq I
, i.e.,
\ell(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})<\ell(I\times S)
, i.e.,
\ell(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})\leq\ell(I\times S)-1
. Now assume
\nu^{\prime}=(\nu_{t}^{\prime},\nu_{\bm{x}})\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})
. As in
4.10
above, one can show that
\nu^{\prime}
does not
“hang in time”
. Therefore, there is
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu^{\prime})
such that
\nu^{\prime}\in\partial I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}
, i.e.,
\nu_{t}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime})
but
\nu_{\bm{x}}\notin\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime})
according to
4.5
. Without loss of generality, we may further assume
S^{\prime}\in\textup{BISECT}(S^{\prime\prime},d)
due to
4.6
. The latter lemma also tells us that
\nu_{\bm{x}}\notin\mathring{S^{\prime\prime}}
, otherwise
\nu^{\prime}
would be a free node. Since,
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\partial S^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime})
,
\nu_{\bm{x}}\notin\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime})
, and
S^{\prime}\in\textup{BISECT}(S^{\prime\prime},d)
, we can conclude that
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\textup{RE}(S^{\prime\prime})
, the refinement edge of
S^{\prime\prime}
.

Furthermore, without loss of generality we may assume
t^{\prime\prime}>t^{\prime}
for
(t^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime})\in I^{\prime\prime}\times I^{\prime}
. Similarly to the proof of
4.6
, we then define
\mathcal{T}_{up}:=\mathcal{T}(v_{t}^{\prime},\mathcal{P})
and
\mathcal{T}_{down}:=\mathcal{T}\left(v_{t}^{\prime}-\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{2},\mathcal{P}\right)
, where
\varepsilon_{0}
is chosen as in the aforementioned proof. Additionally, we choose a
S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{down}
with
|S\cap S^{\prime\prime\prime}|>0
and
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in S^{\prime\prime\prime}
as well as a corresponding interval
I^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that
\nu^{\prime}\in\overline{I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}}
and
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}
. It holds that
\ell(I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime})>\ell(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})
, since otherwise, if
\ell(I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime})\leq\ell(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})
, then
I^{\prime}\subset I^{\prime\prime\prime}
must hold, and therefore
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}=I\times S
. But this would be a contradiction to
I^{\prime}\subset I^{\prime\prime\prime}=I\subsetneq I^{\prime}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\ell(I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime})\geq\ell(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})+1=\ell(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})+2,

(10)

where we have applied
S^{\prime}\in\textup{BISECT}(S^{\prime\prime},d)
in the second step.

Illustration of the prisms in the proof of
4.11
.

Now if
|S^{\prime\prime\prime}\cap S^{\prime\prime}|>0
was true, this would also yield a contradiction to the above equation for the levels due to the
1
-irregular rule in time. Thus,
\dim(S^{\prime\prime}\cap S^{\prime\prime\prime})\in\{0,\dots,d-1\}
. Now let us consider
S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{up}
with
|S^{\prime\prime}\cap S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}|>0
and
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
together with an interval
I^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
such that
I^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}
and
\nu^{\prime}\in\overline{I^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}
. Then, since
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\textup{RE}(S^{\prime\prime})\cap S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
, we have
\textup{RE}(S^{\prime\prime})\subset S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
(
\nu_{\bm{x}}
cannot be a vertex of
\textup{RE}(S^{\prime\prime})
, since this would imply
\nu_{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{L}(S^{\prime\prime})
, which does not hold true), which implies
\ell(I^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime\prime})\leq\ell(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})
according to
3.8
. But again, together with (
10
) this yields another contradiction. Therefore, this finally yields the assertion.
∎

We are now in a position to prove
4.3
.

Proof of
4.3
.

We first consider the case
d=1
. Let
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})
for
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\setminus\omega^{2}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)
. If
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
, then
\nu\neq\nu^{\prime}
, otherwise
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)\subset\omega^{2}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)
due to
\nu^{\prime}\in(\overline{I\times S})\cap(\overline{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}})
, which would be a contradiction. Then
\phi_{\nu}(\nu^{\prime})=0
according to
4.2
. Now consider the case of
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})
. According to
4.6
and
4.10
, respectively, there is a
1
-dimensional face
F
of some
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu^{\prime})
with
\nu^{\prime}\in F
and
F\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
. Let
\nu^{\prime\prime}\in F\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})
. Then,
\nu^{\prime\prime}\neq\nu
, since the opposite would imply
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}\in\omega_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)
as before, and therefore
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{2}
, which is a contradiction. Thus,
\phi_{\nu}(\nu^{\prime\prime})=0
. Since
\phi_{\nu}\phantom{}{}_{|F}\in\Pi^{r_{1}}(I^{\prime})
with
\#(F\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}))=r_{1}=\dim\Pi^{r_{1}}(I^{\prime})
or
\phi_{\nu}\phantom{}{}_{|F}\in\Pi^{r_{2}}(S^{\prime})
with
\#(F\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}))=r_{2}=\dim\Pi^{r_{2}}(S^{\prime})
, correspondingly, this yields
\phi_{\nu}\phantom{}{}_{|F}=0
, in particular,
\phi_{\nu}(\nu^{\prime})=0
. Thus,
\phi_{\nu}(\nu^{\prime})=0
for all
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})
and therefore
\phi_{\nu}\phantom{}{}_{|\overline{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}}}=0
, because
\#\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})=\dim\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})
. Since
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\setminus\omega^{2}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)
has been arbitrarily chosen, this yields the assertion.

Now we turn to the situation of
d\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
. The case of
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
is identical to the case
d=1
. So assume
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})
. Similarly, if
\nu^{\prime}

“hangs in time”
, the proof works identically due to
4.10
and
4.11
. If
\nu^{\prime}=(\nu_{t}^{\prime},\nu_{\bm{x}}^{\prime})

“hangs in space”
, it is a little more complicated. Then, there is
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu^{\prime})
as in
4.6
with
\{\nu_{t}^{\prime}\}\times\mathring{S^{\prime\prime}}\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
. Therefore,
\phi_{\nu}(\nu^{\prime\prime})=0
for
\nu^{\prime\prime}\in\{\nu_{t}^{\prime}\}\times\mathring{S^{\prime\prime}}
can be shown as above. Now consider
\nu^{\prime\prime}\in\{\nu_{t}^{\prime}\}\times\partial S^{\prime\prime}
, i.e.,
\nu^{\prime\prime}=(\nu_{t}^{\prime},\nu_{\bm{x}}^{\prime\prime})
. If
\nu^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
nothing changes, thus assume the case
\nu^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{P})
. Then,
\nu^{\prime\prime}

“hangs in time”
according to
4.7
, i.e., there is
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{H}}(\nu^{\prime\prime})
such that
L(\nu^{\prime\prime}):=\overline{I^{\prime\prime\prime}}\times\{\nu_{\bm{x}}^{\prime\prime}\}\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
due to
4.10
and
4.11
. We now know that
I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\omega^{2}_{\mathcal{P}}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})
. Thus, if
\nu^{\prime\prime\prime}\in L(\nu^{\prime\prime})\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime})
fulfilled
\nu^{\prime\prime\prime}=\nu\in\overline{I\times S}
, then
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{3}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)
would hold in contradiction to the assumption. Thus,
\phi_{\nu}(\nu^{\prime\prime\prime})=0
, again due to
4.2
. Since
\phi_{\nu}\phantom{}{}_{|L(\nu^{\prime\prime\prime})}\in\Pi^{r_{1}}(I^{\prime\prime\prime})
and
\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime\prime})\cap L(\nu^{\prime\prime})=\dim\Pi^{r_{1}}(I^{\prime\prime\prime})
, this implies
\phi_{\nu}\phantom{}{}_{|L(\nu^{\prime\prime})}=0
and therefore
\phi_{\nu}(\nu^{\prime\prime})=0
. Since we have therefore shown that
\phi_{\nu}(\nu^{\prime\prime})=0
for all
\nu^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})\cap(\{\nu_{t}\}\times S^{\prime\prime})
, and
\#(\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{\prime\prime})\cap(\{\nu_{t}\}\times S^{\prime\prime}))=\dim\Pi^{r_{2}}(S^{\prime\prime})
, this yields
\phi_{\nu}\phantom{}{}_{|\{\nu_{t}\}\times S^{\prime\prime}}=0
and thus
\phi_{\nu}(\nu^{\prime})=0
. Thus, the first assertion follows as in the case
d=1
.
The second assertion is now an immediate consequence of
3.13
(ii)
.
∎

4.2
The (quasi-)interpolation operator

The goal of this section is to construct an appropriate (quasi-)interpolation operator from
L_{p}(\Omega_{T})
,
p\in(0,\infty]
, to our anisotropic finite element space
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
. We will proceed similar to
[
GM14
, Sect. 3]
. Also, in the following, we will use
j(d)
as in
4.3
.

First, we will consider the Lagrange basis
(b^{I\times S}_{\nu})_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(I\times S)}
of
\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}(I\times S)
with respect to the nodes in
\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
for every
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}
. Further, we will use the biorthogonal dual functions
(\zeta^{I\times S}_{\nu})_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(I\times S)}\subset\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}(I\times S)
with respect to the
L_{2}(I\times S)
-inner product, i.e.,

\displaystyle\int\limits_{I\times S}b^{I\times S}_{\nu}\zeta^{I\times S}_{\nu^{\prime}}\,d(t,\bm{x})=\delta_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\quad\text{for all}\quad\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(I\times S).

(11)

Remark 4.12
.

For every
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}
,
\nu\in\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
, and
p\in(0,\infty]
, we have
\|\zeta^{I\times S}_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\sim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2}}|I\times S|^{\frac{1}{p}-1}
.

Proof.

Let
\hat{I}=[0,1]
and
\hat{S}
be the
d
-dimensional standard simplex. Then, we know that
b^{\hat{I}\times\hat{S}}_{\hat{\nu}}=b^{I\times S}_{\nu}\circ\Phi_{I\times S}
for
\Phi_{I\times S}(t,\bm{x}):=(\Phi_{I}(t),\Phi_{S}(\bm{x}))
and
\hat{\nu}:=\Phi_{I\times S}^{-1}(\nu)
. Further,

\displaystyle\int\limits_{I\times S}b^{I\times S}_{\nu}\left(\frac{|\hat{I}\times\hat{S}|}{|I\times S|}\cdot\zeta^{\hat{I}\times\hat{S}}_{\hat{\nu}}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{I\times S}\right)\,d(t,\bm{x})=\int\limits_{\hat{I}\times\hat{S}}b^{\hat{I}\times\hat{S}}_{\hat{\nu}}\zeta^{\hat{I}\times\hat{S}}_{\hat{\nu}}\,d(\hat{t},\hat{\bm{x}})=\delta_{\hat{\nu},\hat{\nu}^{\prime}}=\delta_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}

holds for any
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
due to
|\det D\Phi_{I\times S}|=\frac{|I\times S|}{|\hat{I}\times\hat{S}|}
. This yields
\zeta^{I\times S}_{\nu}:=\frac{|\hat{I}\times\hat{S}|}{|I\times S|}\cdot\zeta^{\hat{I}\times\hat{S}}_{\hat{\nu}}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{I\times S}
. Additionally,
\|\zeta^{\hat{I}\times\hat{S}}_{\hat{\nu}}\|_{L_{p}(\hat{I}\times\hat{S})}\sim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2}}1
for any such
\hat{\nu}\in\mathcal{L}(\hat{I}\times\hat{S})
. Together, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\zeta^{I\times S}_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}^{p}\sim_{\,d,p}\int\limits_{\hat{I}\times\hat{S}}\left(\frac{1}{|I\times S|^{p}}\left|\zeta^{\hat{I}\times\hat{S}}_{\hat{\nu}}\right|^{p}\right)|I\times S|\,d(\hat{t},\hat{\bm{x}})=|I\times S|^{1-p}\left\|\zeta^{\hat{I}\times\hat{S}}_{\hat{\nu}}\right\|^{p}_{L_{p}(\hat{I}\times\hat{S})}\sim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2}}|I\times S|^{1-p}.

for
p<\infty
, due to
|\hat{I}\times\hat{S}|=|\hat{S}|\sim_{\,d}1
. The case
p=\infty
works analogously with the usual modifications.
∎

Definition 4.13
.

For given
\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
, we choose an arbitrary, but fixed
J\times R:=(J\times R)(\nu)\in\mathcal{P}
with
\nu\in\overline{J\times R}
. Next, we set
\zeta_{\nu}:=\zeta^{(J\times R)(\nu)}_{\nu}
in order to define
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}:L_{1}(\Omega_{T})\rightarrow\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
via

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}(f):=\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})}\left(\int\limits_{(J\times R)(\nu)}f\,\zeta_{\nu}\,d(t,\bm{x})\right)\phi_{\nu}=\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})}(f,\zeta_{\nu})_{L_{2}((J\times R)(\nu))}\phi_{\nu}

Remark 4.14
.

It is easy to see that
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}
is linear and
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}(f)=f
if and only if
f\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
.

Proof.

The linearity is clear. Let
f\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
. Then,
4.2
yields the expansion
f=\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})}f_{\nu}\phi_{\nu}
,
f_{\nu}\in\mathbb{R}
, with respect to the basis
(\phi_{\nu})_{\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})}
of
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
. Due to the basis property, it is sufficient to show
(f,\zeta_{\nu})_{L_{2}((J\times R)(\nu))}=f_{\nu}
for every
\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
in order to obtain
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}(f)=f
. Therefore, let
\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
and observe that
f_{|J\times R}
is an anisotropic polynomial with a representation with respect to
(b^{J\times R}_{\nu^{\prime}})_{\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(J\times R)}
, which forms a basis of
\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}(J\times R)
, i.e.,
f_{|J\times R}=\sum\limits_{\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(J\times R)}c_{\nu^{\prime}}b_{\nu^{\prime}}^{J\times R}
. First, we now obtain

\displaystyle f_{\nu}=\sum\limits_{\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})}f_{\nu}\delta_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}(\nu)=\sum\limits_{\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})}f_{\nu^{\prime}}\phi_{\nu^{\prime}}(\nu)=f(\nu)=\sum\limits_{\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(J\times R)}c_{\nu^{\prime}}b_{\nu^{\prime}}^{J\times R}(\nu)=\sum\limits_{\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(J\times R)}c_{\nu^{\prime}}\delta_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}=c_{\nu},

due to
\nu\in\overline{J\times R}
, which in turn allows us to derive

\displaystyle(f,\zeta_{\nu})_{L_{2}(J\times R)}=\int\limits_{J\times R}f\,\zeta_{\nu}\,d(t,\bm{x})=\sum\limits_{\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(J\times R)}c_{\nu^{\prime}}\int\limits_{J\times R}b_{\nu^{\prime}}^{J\times R}\zeta_{\nu}\,d(t,\bm{x})=\sum\limits_{\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(J\times R)}c_{\nu^{\prime}}\delta_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}=c_{\nu}=f_{\nu}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.15
.

For given
\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})\cap\mathcal{L}(I\times S)
and
p\in(0,\infty]
, we can estimate
\|\phi_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\sim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2}}|\supp\phi_{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{p}}
.

Proof.

Let
M_{\nu}
be the set of all elements of
\mathcal{P}
in the support of
\phi_{\nu}
, i.e.,
M_{\nu}:=\{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\mid I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\subset\supp\phi_{\nu}\}
. As above, we now use the affine mapping
\Phi_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}}
that transforms the reference prism
\hat{I}\times\hat{S}
to
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}
as well as
|\hat{I}\times\hat{S}|\sim_{\,d}1
in order to obtain

\displaystyle\|\phi_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in M_{\nu}}\int\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}}|\phi_{\nu}|^{p}\,d(t,\bm{x})=\sum\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in M_{\nu}}\int\limits_{\hat{I}\times\hat{S}}|\phi_{\nu}\circ\Phi_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}}|^{p}\,\frac{|I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}|}{|\hat{I}\times\hat{S}|}\,d(\hat{t},\hat{\bm{x}})

\displaystyle\sim_{\,d}\sum\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in M_{\nu}}|I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}|\int\limits_{\hat{I}\times\hat{S}}|\phi_{\nu}\circ\Phi_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}}|^{p}\,d(\hat{t},\hat{\bm{x}})\sim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2}}\sum\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in M_{\nu}}|I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}|=|\supp\phi_{\nu}|,

if
p<\infty
. The case
p=\infty
can be treated similarly.
∎

Together, this allows us to show the following result concerning the local boundedness of
\mathcal{Q}(f)
.

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty]
and
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}
. Then

(i)

\|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\lesssim_{\,d,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f\|_{L_{p}(\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S))}\quad\text{for all}\quad f\in L_{p}(\Omega_{T}),\quad\text{if}\quad p\geq 1
10
10

10

With a little abuse of notation, we denote
\|f\|_{L_{p}(\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S))}:=\left(\sum\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}\|f\|_{L_{p}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}
with the usual modification for
p=\infty
.
.

(ii)

\|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\lesssim_{\,d,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f\|_{L_{p}(\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S))}\quad\text{for all}\quad f\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P},\textup{DC}}.

Proof.

We will only provide the details of the proof for the case
p<\infty
, since
p=\infty
is very similar. First, we assume
f\in L_{p}(\Omega_{T})
and notice that

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}(f)_{|I\times S}=\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})}(f,\zeta_{\nu})_{L_{2}((J\times R)(\nu))}\phi_{\nu}\phantom{}{}_{|I\times S}=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P}),\\
I\times S\subset\supp\phi_{\nu}\end{subarray}}(f,\zeta_{\nu})_{L_{2}((J\times R)(\nu))}\phi_{\nu}.

By
4.3
as well as the Minkowski and Hölder inequalities, this allows us to estimate

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\leq\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}),\\
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)-1}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)\end{subarray}}\|f\|_{L_{p}((J\times R)(\nu))}\|\zeta_{\nu}\|_{L_{p^{\prime}}((J\times R)(\nu))}\|\phi_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)},

(12)

where
p^{\prime}\in[1,\infty]
is chosen such that
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}=1
. Additionally, we have

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{\nu}\|_{L_{p^{\prime}}((J\times R)(\nu))}\sim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2}}|(J\times R)(\nu)|^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}-1}\sim_{\,d,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)|^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}-1}

(13)

due to
4.12
and
3.13
(ii)
, since
(J\times R)(\nu)\in\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)
for such
\nu
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\|\phi_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\sim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2}}|\supp\phi_{\nu}|^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq|\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)|^{\frac{1}{p}}

(14)

holds true because of
4.15
and
4.3
. Inserting (
13
) and (
14
) into (
12
) now yields

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}

\displaystyle\sim_{\,d,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}),\\
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)-1}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)\end{subarray}}\|f\|_{L_{p}((J\times R)(\nu))}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}),\\
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)-1}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)\end{subarray}}\|f\|_{L_{p}(\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S))}\lesssim_{\,d,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f\|_{L_{p}(\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S))},

where we have used that
(J\times R)(\nu)\in\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)
for such
\nu
in the penultimate step and the boundedness of
\omega^{j(d)-1}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)
due to
3.13
(ii)
in the last one. This concludes the proof for the case
10
. Now, let us consider
f\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P},\textup{DC}}
, i.e.,
f=\sum\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathds{1}_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}}f_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}}
with
f_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}}\in\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})
for
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}
. Since
\Omega_{T}
is bounded,
(ii)
is a particular case of
10
. So it is sufficient to show the assertion for
p\in(0,1)
only. Similar to before, using the subadditivity of
\|\cdot\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}(f)\|^{p}_{L_{p}(I\times S)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}),\\
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)-1}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)\end{subarray}}|(f,\zeta_{\nu})_{L_{2}((J\times R)(\nu))}|^{p}\,\|\phi_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}),\\
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)-1}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)\end{subarray}}\|f\|_{L_{\infty}((J\times R)(\nu))}^{p}\|\phi_{\nu}\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})\cap\mathcal{L}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}),\\
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)-1}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)\end{subarray}}|\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)|^{-1}\|f\|_{L_{p}((J\times R)(\nu))}^{p}|\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)|

\displaystyle\leq\|f\|_{L_{p}(\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}(I\times S))}^{p},

where we have additionally used the (quasi-)norm equivalency from
[
MSS26
, Lem. 3.5]
together with
3.13
(ii)
and
4.15
in the penultimate step. This shows
(ii)
and concludes the proof.
∎

Since
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}
cannot be applied to arbitrary functions in
L_{p}(\Omega)
, if
p\in(0,1)
, we will have to combine the projection
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}
appropriately with another operator. Therefore, for
D\subset\Omega_{T}
, consider the operator
B_{p,D}:L_{p}(D)\rightarrow\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}(D)
that maps any function to one of its
best approximations
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\|f-B_{p,D}(f)\|_{L_{p}(D)}=E\left(f,\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}(D),D\right)_{p}=:E\left(f,\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},D\right)_{p}.

Remark 4.17
.

For any
p\in(0,\infty]
, the space
L_{p}(\Omega_{T})
is a (quasi-)Banach space. Therefore, a best approximation of any
f\in L_{p}(\Omega_{T})
in the finite dimensional space
\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}
exists, i.e.,
\mathcal{B}_{p,D}
is well-defined. In the Banach space case, i.e.,
p\in[1,\infty]
, this is a direct consequence of Theorem 1.1 of
[
DL93
, Ch. 3.1]
. For
p\in(0,1)
, this follows by minor modifications of the proof of the aforementioned theorem, adjusting it to the case of quasi-Banach spaces.

In particular, for
p\in(0,1]\cup\{\infty\}
,
\mathcal{B}_{p,D}
might not be continuous, since there can be multiple best approximations, because
\|\cdot\|_{L_{p}(D)}
is not (strictly) convex.

We will need the following property, that tells us that best-approximations with respect to some small parameter are also quasi-best approximations for the larger parameters. The corresponding result for the stationary case can be found in
[
GM14
, Lem. 3.5]
.

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty]
,
\rho\in(0,p]
, and
f\in L_{\rho}(\Omega_{T})
. Further, assume
D=I\times S
or
D=\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)
for some
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}
. Then,

\displaystyle\|f-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,D}(f)\|_{L_{p}(D)}\lesssim E\left(f,\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},D\right)_{p},

where the constant only depends on
d,\rho,p,r_{1},r_{2}
in the first case and on
d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
, and
\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
in the latter.

Proof.

This can be shown analogously to the corresponding proof in
[
DP88
, Lem. 3.2]
. Following the lines of the proof, one can see that the involved constant depends on
\rho
,
p
, and the constant
C
corresponding to the (quasi-)norm equivalency estimate

\displaystyle\|Q\|_{L_{p}(D)}\leq C\,|D|^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\rho}}\|Q\|_{L_{\rho}(D)}\quad\text{for all}\quad Q\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}.

The latter has to hold on any bounded
D
, since
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
is finite dimensional and thus all (quasi-)norms must be equivalent. Note that the estimate is invariant under affine-linear transformations. Therefore, for
D=I\times S
, the assertion follows directly by transforming
I\times S
to the standard prism
\hat{I}\times\hat{S}
, where the estimate has to hold with a constant depending only on
d
,
r_{1}
, and
r_{2}
. Now, consider the case of
D=\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)
and
p<\infty
. Then,

\displaystyle\|Q\|^{p}_{L_{p}(\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S))}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}\|Q\|^{p}_{L_{p}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})}\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,r_{1},r_{2}}\sum\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}|I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}|^{1-\frac{p}{\rho}}\|Q\|_{L_{\rho}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)|^{1-\frac{p}{\rho}}\sum\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}\|Q\|_{L_{\rho}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})}^{p},

\displaystyle\leq|\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)|^{1-\frac{p}{\rho}}\left(\sum\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}\|Q\|_{L_{\rho}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})}^{\rho}\right)^{\frac{p}{\rho}}=|\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)|^{1-\frac{p}{\rho}}\|Q\|^{p}_{L_{\rho}(\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S))},

where we have used the result for
D=I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}
in the second step,
3.13
(ii)
in the third, and the embedding for the sequence spaces
\ell^{\rho}(\mathbb{N}_{0})\rightarrow\ell^{p}(\mathbb{N}_{0})
in the penultimate one. This shows the assertion, since the case of
p=\infty
works similar with the usual modifications.
∎

Consequently, for any
p\in(0,\infty]
, we can define
\mathcal{B}_{p,\mathcal{P}}:=\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}}\mathds{1}_{I\times S}\mathcal{B}_{p,I\times S}\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P},\textup{DC}}
and show a corresponding localized best approximation estimate.

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty]
,
\rho\in(0,p]
,
f\in L_{p}(\Omega_{T})
, and
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}
. Then

\displaystyle\|f-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S))}\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}E\left(f,\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)\right)_{p}

Proof.

For
p<\infty
, by using the best-approximation property of
\mathcal{B}_{p,\cdot}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|f-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S))}^{p}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}\|f-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\sum\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}\|f-\mathcal{B}_{p,\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})}^{p}

\displaystyle=\|f-\mathcal{B}_{{p},\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}(I\times S))}^{p}=E\left(f,\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)\right)^{p}_{p},

by using the result of
4.18
in the second step, where
\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}:=\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\big(\bigcup\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\big)}
. The case
p=\infty
works similarly, replacing the sums by suprema.
∎

This leads to the following definition of the
quasi-interpolation operator
on
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
.

Definition 4.20
.

For
p\in(0,\infty]
, we define
\pi_{p,\mathcal{P}}:L_{p}(\Omega)\rightarrow\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
via
\pi_{p,\mathcal{P}}:=\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\mathcal{B}_{p,\mathcal{P}}
.

For this operator, we can show that
\pi_{p,\mathcal{P}}
also satisfies a local boundedness estimate, analogous to the stationary estimate in
[
GM14
, Lem. 3.13]
, which will be a key ingredient in the proof of the direct estimates.

Theorem 4.21
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty]
,
\rho\in(0,p]
,
f\in L_{\rho}(\Omega_{T})
, and
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}
. Then

\displaystyle\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}E\left(f,\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)\right)_{p}

Proof.

Again, we will only present a detailed proof for the case
p\in(0,\infty)
, the case
p=\infty
is similar. We estimate

\displaystyle\|f-

\displaystyle\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,p}\|f-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}+\|\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}(f)-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}

\displaystyle=\|f-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}+\|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}(f)-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f))\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}+\|\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}(f)-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S))}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,p}\|f-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S))}+\|f-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S))}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}E\left(f,\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)\right)_{p},

where we have applied the result of
4.14
in the second step,
4.16
(ii)
in the third, and
4.18
as well as
4.19
in the last one.
∎

Also, in the next theorem, we show that the operator
\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)
is a
global quasi-best approximation
of
f
with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}
,
0<\rho\leq p
, in the finite element space
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
. This will be crucial for obtaining inverse estimates.

Theorem 4.22
.

Consider
p\in(0,\infty]
,
\rho\in(0,p]
, and
f\in L_{\rho}(\Omega_{T})
. Then,

\displaystyle\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}E\left(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}},\Omega_{T}\right)_{p}

Proof.

First, we observe that by
4.18
,
\|f-\mathcal{B}_{p,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,r_{1},r_{2}}E\left(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P},\textup{DC}},\Omega_{T}\right)_{p}
holds true and additionally consider
G\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
such that
\|f-G\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}=E\left(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}},\Omega_{T}\right)_{p}
, which has to exist, since
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
is finite dimensional. We proceed similar to the proof of
4.21
and derive

\displaystyle\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,p}\|f-G\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}+\|G-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle=E\left(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}},\Omega_{T}\right)_{p}+\|\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}(G-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f))\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}E\left(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}},\Omega_{T}\right)_{p}+\|G-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,}E\left(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}},\Omega_{T}\right)_{p}+\|f-G\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}+\|f-\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,r_{1},r_{2}}E\left(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}},\Omega_{T}\right)_{p},

where we have applied
4.14
in the second step,
4.16
(ii)
together with the finite overlap of the neighborhood domains due to
3.13
(iii)
in the third, and
E\left(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P},\textup{DC}},\Omega_{T}\right)_{p}\leq E\left(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}},\Omega_{T}\right)_{p}
in the last one.
∎

Further, we will need an extension of the
Whitney estimate
from
[
MSS26
, Thm. 1.2]
to the extended neighborhoods of cylindrical domains in the next section. Therefore, we proceed similar to
[
GM14
, Sect. 4.4]
.

Lemma 4.23
.

Let
p,q\in(0,\infty]
as well as
s_{1},s_{2}\in(0,\infty)
such that
\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}>0
and
s_{i}<r_{i}
for some
r_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
,
i=1,2
. Additionally, let
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}
and
f\in B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})
. Then,

\displaystyle E\Big(f,\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},\widetilde{\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}}(I\times S)\Big)_{p}\lesssim_{\,d,p,q,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|I\times S|^{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}}|f|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}(\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S))}

The assertion still holds true if we replace
|f|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}(\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S))}
by
|f|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}(\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S))}^{(r_{1},r_{2}),*}
.

Proof.

It is clearly sufficient to show this for the case of
r_{i}:=\max(\lfloor s_{i}\rfloor+1,2)
. Since
\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S)
is a cylindrical domain, we put
\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S):=J\times R
with an interval
J
and a (spatial) Lipschitz domain
R\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
11
11

11

Of course, technically this means
\bigcup\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S)}I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}=J\times R
.
Further, consider the
reference domain

R^{ref}:=\frac{1}{|R|^{\frac{1}{d}}}R
. Using the bijective, affine transformation
\Phi_{J\times R}:[0,1]\times R^{ref}\rightarrow J\times R
we have

\displaystyle|J\times R|^{-\frac{1}{q}}\,\min\left(|J|^{s_{1}},|R|^{\frac{s_{2}}{d}}\right)|f|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}(J\times R)}

\displaystyle\leq|\hat{f}|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R^{ref})}

\displaystyle\leq|J\times R|^{-\frac{1}{q}}\,\max\left(|J|^{s_{1}},|R|^{\frac{s_{2}}{d}}\right)|f|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}(J\times R)}

(15)

for
\hat{f}:=f\circ\Phi_{J\times R}
, as in the proof of
[
MSS26
, Lem. 3.7]
. Since

\displaystyle|J|\sim_{\,d,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|I^{\prime}|\quad\text{and}\quad|R|\sim_{\,d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|S^{\prime}|\quad\text{for any }I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\text{ with }I^{\prime}\subset J\text{ and }S^{\prime}\subset R,

(16)

due to
3.13
(ii)
, as well as
a(\mathcal{P})\lesssim a(\mathcal{P}_{0})
according to the properties of the atomic refinement method, see
3.1
6
, we can estimate

\displaystyle\min\left(|J|^{s_{1}},|R|^{\frac{s_{2}}{d}}\right)\sim_{\,d,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|J\times R|^{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\sim_{\,d,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\max\left(|J|^{s_{1}},|R|^{\frac{s_{2}}{d}}\right)

(17)

as in the beginning of the proof of
[
MSS26
, Thm. 1.2]
. Also, by the Jacobi transformation theorem,

\displaystyle E\left(f,\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},J\times R\right)_{p}=|J\times R|^{\frac{1}{p}}\,E\left(\hat{f},\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},[0,1]\times R^{ref}\right)_{p}.

(18)

Together, (
16
), (
17
), (
18
), and
|I\times S|\sim_{\,d,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|J\times R|
, due to
3.13
(ii)
, show that the assertion follows if

\displaystyle E\left(\hat{f},\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},[0,1]\times R^{ref}\right)_{p}\lesssim_{\,\,d,p,q,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|\hat{f}|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R^{ref})}

(19)

can be shown for any reference domain
[0,1]\times R^{ref}
.

First, the Lipschitz properties of
R^{ref}
and its diameter are determined by
d
,
\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
, and
\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
. Therefore, according to the anisotropic Jackson’s estimate from
[
MSS26
, Thm. 1.1]
,
2.1
(i)
and
(iii)
, it exists
\hat{P}\in\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}(J\times R^{ref})
with

\displaystyle\|\hat{f}-\hat{P}\|_{L_{q}([0,1]\times R^{ref})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,q,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\omega_{r_{1},t}(\hat{f},[0,1]\times R^{ref},1)_{q}+\omega_{r_{2},\bm{x}}(\hat{f},[0,1]\times R^{ref},\textup{diam}(R^{ref}))_{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,q}|\hat{f}|^{*}_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R^{ref})}\lesssim_{\,d,q,s_{1},s_{2},d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|\hat{f}|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R^{ref})},

(20)

where we have used the equivalency of different anisotropic Besov (quasi-)seminorms from (
3
) in the last step (together with
2.1
(ii)
in the penultimate step, if
s_{i}\in(0,1)
for one
i=1,2
). Further, the continuous embedding
B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})\hookrightarrow L_{p}(\Omega_{T})
from
[
MSS26
, Thm. 1.3]
has an embedding constant depending only on
d,p,q,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
, and
\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
, since
\textup{diam}(Q)\sim_{d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|[0,1]\times R^{ref}|=1
for every subprism
Q
of
R^{ref}
. Therefore, additionally using the (quasi-)norm equivalency mentioned at the end of
Section

2.1.2
, if
s_{i}\in(0,1)
for any
i=1,2
, while keeping in mind that
\textup{LipProp}(R^{ref})
is determined by
d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
, and
\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
, we obtain

\displaystyle E\left(\hat{f},\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},[0,1]\times R^{ref}\right)_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\|\hat{f}-\hat{P}\|_{L_{p}([0,1]\times R^{ref})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,q,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|\hat{f}-\hat{P}\|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R^{ref})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,q,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|\hat{f}-\hat{P}\|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R^{ref})}^{(r_{1},r_{2})}

\displaystyle=\|\hat{f}-\hat{P}\|_{L_{q}([0,1]\times R^{ref})}+|\hat{f}-\hat{P}|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R^{ref})}^{(r_{1},r_{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,q,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|\hat{f}|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R^{ref})},

where we have used (
4.2
) and the fact that
|\hat{P}|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R^{ref})}^{(r_{1},r_{2})}=0
because of
\hat{P}\in\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}([0,1]\times R^{ref})
(this is a consequence of
[
MSS26
, Lem. 2.16]
) together with
2.1
(ii)
if any
s_{i}\in(0,1)
,
i=1,2
, in the last step. A careful read of the above proof shows, that the version of the assertion with
|f|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}(\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S))}^{(r_{1},r_{2}),*}
instead of
|f|_{B^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}(\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S))}
has also been shown.
∎

5
Almost characterization of approximation classes with respect to space-time finite elements

We are now in a position to apply the results of the previous sections to prove the main results of this article, i.e., an almost characterization of approximation classes for approximation with anisotropic finite elements. Throughout this section, we assume
\mathcal{P}_{0}
to be a tensor product structure mesh covering
\Omega_{T}
as described in the beginning of
Section

3
and
j(d):=2
for
d=1
, but
j(d):=3
if
d\geq 2
as in
4.3
.

5.1
Multiscale decomposition

Let us assume that
\mathcal{P}
has been derived from
\mathcal{P}_{0}
by finitely many applications of
\textup{PATCH}\_\textup{REFINE}(\cdot,\cdot,d,s_{1},s_{2})
for any given
s_{1},s_{2}\in(0,\infty)
. Since we want to study how well functions can be approximated with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}
for
p\in(0,\infty]
and
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in(0,\infty)
by anisotropic polynomials, we first have to determine in which cases the approximants are in an anisotropic Besov space. This is done in the following lemma, similarly to
[
GM14
, Prop. 4.7]
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
r_{1},r_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
be the temporal and spatial polynomial degrees with
\alpha_{i}<r_{i}
,
i=1,2
. Then
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}\subset B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}
if and only if
\max(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})<1+\frac{1}{p}
.

Proof.

Let
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}
,
\nu\in\mathcal{N}(I\times S)\cap\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
, and
\phi_{\nu}\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
the basis function corresponding to
\nu
. Further, we set
\Theta_{\nu}:=\supp\phi_{\nu}\subset\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}(I\times S)
, which is bounded due to
4.3
. Additionally, we consider the temporal and spatial diameter of
\Theta_{\nu}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\textup{diam}_{t}\left(\Theta_{\nu}\right):=\sup_{(t,\bm{x}),(t^{\prime},\bm{x}^{\prime})\in\Theta_{\nu}}|t-t^{\prime}|\quad\text{and}\quad\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}\left(\Theta_{\nu}\right):=\sup_{(t,\bm{x}),(t^{\prime},\bm{x}^{\prime})\in\Theta_{\nu}}|\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{\prime}|.

(21)

Since
\phi_{\nu}(\nu)=1
and
\phi_{\nu}\equiv 0
outside of
\Theta_{\nu}
,
\phi_{\nu}(t,\cdot)
and
\phi_{\nu}(\cdot,\bm{x})
are Lipschitz continuous with respect to the constants
\Lip(\phi_{\nu}(t,\cdot))
and
\Lip(\phi_{\nu}(\cdot,\bm{x}))
, where

\displaystyle\Lip(\phi_{\nu}(t,\cdot))\sim_{d,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\textup{diam}_{t}\left(\Theta_{\nu}\right)^{-1}\quad\text{and}\quad\Lip(\phi_{\nu}(\cdot,\bm{x}))\sim_{d,s_{1},s_{2},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}\left(\Theta_{\nu}\right)^{-1}

for any
t\in[0,T]
and
\bm{x}\in\overline{\Omega}
. Therefore, using the algebraic expressions for the difference operators from
[
MSS26
, Rem. 2.2]
, we obtain

\displaystyle|\Delta^{r_{1}}_{h,t}\phi_{\nu}(t,\bm{x})|+|\Delta^{r_{2}}_{\bm{h},\bm{x}}\phi_{\nu}(t,\bm{x})|\sim_{d,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\min\left(1,|h|\textup{diam}_{t}(\Theta_{\nu})^{-1}\right)+\min\left(1,|\bm{h}|\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}(\Theta_{\nu})^{-1}\right)

(22)

for
h\in\mathbb{R}
,
\bm{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
t\in[0,T]_{r_{1},h}
, and
\bm{x}\in\overline{\Omega}_{r_{2},\bm{h}}
. Further, this equivalence implies

\displaystyle|\supp(\Delta^{r_{1}}_{h,t}\phi_{\nu})|\sim_{\,r_{1}}|\supp(\Delta^{r_{1}}_{h,t}\phi_{\nu})\cap\supp(\phi_{\nu})|\quad\text{and}\quad|\supp(\Delta^{r_{2}}_{\bm{h},\bm{x}}\phi_{\nu})|\sim_{\,r_{2}}|\supp(\Delta^{r_{2}}_{\bm{h},\bm{x}}\phi_{\nu})\cap\supp(\phi_{\nu})|.

(23)

Additionally, we know that
\Delta^{r_{1}}_{h,t}P=\Delta^{r_{2}}_{\bm{h},\bm{x}}P=0
for
P\in\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}
due to
[
MSS26
, Lem. 2.16]
. Therefore,
\Delta^{r_{1}}_{h,t}\phi_{\nu}=0
on
\bigcup\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\Theta_{\nu}}\{t\in I^{\prime}\mid d(t,\partial I^{\prime})\geq r_{1}h\}\times S^{\prime}
and
\Delta^{r_{2}}_{\bm{h},\bm{x}}\phi_{\nu}=0
on
\bigcup\limits_{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\Theta_{\nu}}I^{\prime}\times\{\bm{x}\in{S^{\prime}}\mid d(\bm{x},\partial S^{\prime})\geq r_{2}\bm{h}\}
. Thus,

\displaystyle|\supp(\Delta^{r_{1}}_{h,t}\phi_{\nu})\cap\supp(\phi_{\nu})|\sim_{d,r_{1},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}(\Theta_{\nu})^{d}\min(|h|,\textup{diam}_{t}(\Theta_{\nu}))

(24)

and

\displaystyle|\supp(\Delta^{r_{2}}_{\bm{h},\bm{x}}\phi_{\nu})\cap\supp(\phi_{\nu})|\sim_{d,r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\textup{diam}_{t}(\Theta_{\nu})\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}(\Theta_{\nu})^{d-1}\min(|\bm{h}|,\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}(\Theta_{\nu})).

(25)

For
\delta\in\min(\textup{diam}_{t}(\Theta_{\nu}),\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}(\Theta_{\nu}))
, (
22
), (
23
), (
24
), and (
25
) together yield

\displaystyle\omega_{r_{1},t}(\phi_{\nu},\Omega_{T},\delta)_{p}^{q}+\omega_{r_{2},\bm{x}}(\phi_{\nu},\Omega_{T},\delta)_{p}^{q}\sim_{d,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\left(\delta^{1+\frac{1}{p}}\right)^{q}=\left(\delta^{q+\frac{q}{p}}\right),

which is equivalent to
|\phi_{\nu}|^{(r_{1},r_{2})}_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}<\infty
and thus
\phi_{\nu}\in B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
(due to the (quasi-)norm equivalency for Besov (quasi-)norms from
Section

2.1.2
and
r_{i}>\alpha_{i}
,
i=1,2
). Therefore,
\phi_{\nu}\in B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
if and only if
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}<1+\frac{1}{p}
.
∎

Now since
F\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
can be approximated up to an error zero with respect to the adaptive anisotropic finite element method and any error (quasi-)norm, but
F\notin B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
if
\max(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\geq 1+\frac{1}{p}
, we additionally need a generalized notion of anisotropic Besov spaces for the derivation of inverse estimates. Namely, we want to show that if a function can be well approximated with the adaptive technique, it necessarily possesses some (generalized) Besov regularity of higher order.

This generalized notion of Besov regularity, can also be interpreted as a different characterization of the original anisotropic Besov spaces (defined in
Section

2.1.2
) for
0<\max(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})<1+\frac{1}{p}
, which is also of value when proving Sobolev-type embeddings in our anisotropic Besov scale and corresponding direct estimates. This is why we want to introduce it before the specific sections on direct and inverse estimates, i.e.,
Section

5.2
and
Section

5.3
, respectively. Its relevance is motivated by the result on multiscale decompositions stated in
5.5
, which corresponds to
[
GM14
, Sect. 4.3 and Sect. 7]
.

Assumption 5.2
.

Choose
s_{1},s_{2}\in(0,\infty)
. For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\mathcal{P}_{n}
be derived from
\mathcal{P}_{n-1}
via uniform refinement of all elements by the method
\textup{ATOMIC}\_\textup{SPLIT}(\cdot,d,s_{1},s_{2})
.
12
12

12

\mathcal{P}_{n}
could also be interpreted as a result of
\textup{MARKED}\_\textup{REFINE}(\mathcal{P}_{0},d,s_{1},s_{2})
with respect to a MARK-routine that marks prisms for refinement if and only if they have a level strictly less than
n
.
Further, assume
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\in\mathbb{R}(s_{1},s_{2})
and
r_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
for
i=1,2
, and set
\pi_{n}:=\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}_{n}}
(with respect to
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
) for given
\rho\in(0,p]
and
p,q\in(0,\infty]
. Now put
\Delta_{n}:=\pi_{n}-\pi_{n-1}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
\pi_{-1}:=0
, and define

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{\Delta}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}nq}\|\Delta_{n}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}},&q<\infty\\
\ \ \sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}2^{\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}n}\|\Delta_{n}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}\ \ \ \,,&q=\infty\end{array}\right\}

for any
f\in L_{q}(\Omega)
.
Note
that there is a hidden dependency of
\|\cdot\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{\Delta}
on the choice of
\rho
and
(r_{1},r_{2})
. We will not indicate this in the sequel in order to improve readability.

Remark 5.3
.

The case of
\alpha_{i}\leq 0
,
i=1,2
, will first be of relevance in
Section

5.3
. Before, we will mainly consider positive values for
\alpha_{i}
,
i=1,2
.

Remark 5.4
.

Notice that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{n}=\bigcup\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{0}}\textup{ATOMIC}\_\textup{SPLIT}(\cdot,d,s_{1},s_{2})^{\otimes n}=\left(\bigcup\limits_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}\textup{BISECT}(1,I)^{\otimes\left\lceil\frac{ns_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right\rceil}\right)\otimes\left(\bigcup_{S\in\mathcal{T}_{0}}\textup{BISECT}(d,S)^{\otimes n}\right),

i.e.,
\mathcal{P}_{n}
is itself a tensor product mesh. Therefore, it is uniform, since its spatial triangulation is uniform due to
[
Ste08
, Thm. 4.3]
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
5.2
hold with
0<\alpha_{i}<r_{i}
,
i=1,2
, and
f\in B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
. Then,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{\Delta}\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),\textup{LipProp}(\Omega)}\|f\|_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}.

Proof.

We assume
q<\infty
, the proof for
q=\infty
is very similar. Further, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{n}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\lesssim_{\,p}\|f-\pi_{n}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}+\|f-\pi_{n-1}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}.

(26)

Additionally,
\mathcal{P}_{n}
is conforming due to the uniform refinement, which implies
\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I\times S)=\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S)
. Therefore, we can denote any
j(d)
-th neighborhood as a cylindrical domain
J\times R=\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}_{\max(n-1,0)}}(I\times S)
,
J\subset[0,T]
an interval,
R\subset\Omega
, with the Lipschitz properties of
R
determined by
d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
, and
\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
. Further, as in
3.13
(ii)
, we can show
|J|\sim_{d,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|I|\sim_{\,\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-\frac{\max(n-1,0)\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}}
and
|R|\sim_{d,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|S|\sim_{\,\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-\max(n-1,0)}
. This enables us to estimate

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{n}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,p}\|f-\pi_{n}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}+\|f-\pi_{n-1}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{N}}(n)+\|f\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\,\mathds{1}_{\{0\}}(n)

(27)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}E\left(f,\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},J\times R\right)_{p}+\|f\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\,\mathds{1}_{\{0\}}(n)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\omega_{r_{1},t}\left(f,J\times R,|J|\right)_{p}+\omega_{r_{2},\bm{x}}\left(f,J\times R,\textup{diam}(R)\right)_{p}+\|f\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\,\mathds{1}_{\{0\}}(n)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\mathrm{w}_{r_{1},t}\left(f,J\times R,\frac{|J|}{4r_{1}}\right)_{p}+\mathrm{w}_{r_{2},\bm{x}}\left(f,J\times R,\delta_{n}\right)_{p}+\|f\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\,\mathds{1}_{\{0\}}(n)

where we have subsequently applied
4.21
, Jackson’s estimate from
[
MSS26
, Thm. 1.1]
, and
2.1
(iii)
together with the equivalency between suprema and averaged moduli of smoothness from
[
MSS26
, Lem. 2.7 and Lem. 2.8]
. Here,
0<\delta_{n}=\delta_{n}(r_{2},d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}))\leq 2^{-\frac{n}{d}}
is a parameter that is uniformly bounded from below for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
due to the fact that the Lipschitz properties are determined by the parameters
d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
, and
\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
. The observations from
2.1
(i)
,
(iii)
, and
(iv)
together with the finite overlap of the neighborhood domains, due to
3.13
(ii)
, now yields

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{n}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\mathrm{w}_{r_{1},t}\left(f,\Omega_{T},\frac{|J|}{4r_{1}}\right)_{p}+\mathrm{w}_{r_{2},\bm{x}}\left(f,\Omega_{T},\delta_{n}\right)_{p}+\|f\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}\,\mathds{1}_{\{0\}}(n)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2}}\omega_{r_{1},t}\left(f,\Omega_{T},2^{-\frac{n\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}}\right)_{p}+\omega_{r_{2},\bm{x}}\left(f,\Omega_{T},2^{-\frac{n}{d}}\right)_{p}+\|f\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}\,\mathds{1}_{\{0\}}(n)

(28)

for
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, by summing over all
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}
. Thus, the assertion

\displaystyle\|f\|^{\Delta}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f\|_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{*,(r_{1},r_{2})}\sim_{\,d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\textup{LipProp}(\Omega)}\|f\|_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}.

follows, where we have applied the (quasi-)norm equivalency result mentioned below (
3
) in the last step.
∎

This lemma leads to the following definition.

Definition 5.6
.

Given
5.2
, we define
generalized anisotropic Besov spaces
via multiscale decomposition, i.e.,

\displaystyle\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T}):=\left\{f\in L_{p}(\Omega_{T})\ \bigg|\ \|f\|^{\Delta}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}<\infty\right\}.

Remark 5.7
.

(i)

These spaces indeed generalize the notion of the anisotropic Besov spaces, since
5.5
implies
B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})\hookrightarrow\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
for
0<\alpha_{i}<r_{i}
,
i=1,2
. Further, they are suited for our analysis, since for every
\mathcal{P}
derived from
\mathcal{P}_{0}
through finitely man applications of
\textup{PATCH}\_\textup{REFINE}(\cdot,\cdot,d,s_{1},s_{2})
, there exists some
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathcal{P}_{n}
is a sub-partition of
\mathcal{P}
and thus,
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}\subset\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{m}}
for any
m\geq n
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
. The latter, in particular, yields
\Delta_{m}(F)=0
for every
F\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
and
m>n
, i.e.,
F\in\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
. Together with the result of
5.1
, this shows, that
B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})\subsetneq\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
, if
\max(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\geq 1+\frac{1}{p}
in the aforementioned case of
0<\alpha_{i}<r_{i}
,
i=1,2
. Lastly, further below in
5.13
, we will see that in this case, i.e.,
0<\alpha_{i}<r_{i}
,
i=1,2
, the classical and generalized Besov spaces indeed coincide in the sense of equivalent (quasi-)norms, if the smoothness parameters are small, i.e.,
\max(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})<1+\frac{1}{p}
.

(ii)

Let
f\in\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})\subset L_{p}(\Omega_{T})
, then
\Delta_{n}(f)\in V^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Further, consider the basis
\left(\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}\right)_{\nu\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P}_{n})}
of
V^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}
, according to
4.2
. Additionally, recall that
