\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})=\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P}_{n})
because of the conformity of
\mathcal{P}_{n}
. Then, there are real coefficients
b_{\nu}^{(n)}
, such that

\displaystyle f=\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\Delta_{n}(f)=\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})}\left(b_{\nu}^{(n)}(f)\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}\right)\quad\text{in}\quad L_{q}(\Omega_{T}),

which is a
multiscale decomposition
of
f
. Be attentive to the hidden dependency of
b_{\nu}^{(n)}(f)
and
\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}
on the polynomial orders
(r_{1},r_{2})
.

(iii)

Furthermore, due to the conformity of
\mathcal{P}_{n}
according to
5.4
,
\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j}(I\times S)=\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j}}(I\times S)
for any
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
, and

\displaystyle\supp\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}=\{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}\mid\nu\in\overline{I\times S}\}=:\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}(\nu)\subset\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}(I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})

for any
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}
with
\nu\in\overline{I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}}
. Since the proof from
3.13
(iii)
in this situation even yields
\#\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}(I\times S)\lesssim_{\,d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}}1
for any
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}
, this implies

\#\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}(\nu)\lesssim_{\,d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}}1
.
Furthermore, this also shows that
|I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}|\sim_{\,d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\left|\supp\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}\right|\sim_{\,d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-n\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)}
for any
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}
with
I^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}\subset\supp\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}
.

(iv)

Additionally, we can estimate, very similarly to
3.13
(ii)
, that

\displaystyle\textup{diam}_{t}\left(\supp\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}\right)\sim_{\,d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-\frac{ns_{2}}{s_{1}d}}\quad\text{and}\quad\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}\left(\supp\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}\right)\sim_{\,d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-\frac{n}{d}},

where the notation has been borrowed from the proof of
5.1
, in particular, (
21
).

From now on, we will study and apply multiscale decompositions to investigate their relation to (generalized) anisotropic Besov spaces. In order to do so, we will need two lemmata, similar to
[
DP88
, Lem. 4.1 and Lem. 4.2]
.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
5.2
hold true,
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
F\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}
, and
F=\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})}c_{\nu}\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}
be the representation of
F
with respect to the basis
\left(\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}\right)_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})}
of
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}
. Then,

\displaystyle|c_{\nu}|\lesssim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{\frac{n}{p}\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)}\|F\|_{L_{p}\left(\supp\phi^{(n)}_{\nu}\right)}.

Proof.

First, we estimate
|c_{\nu}|\leq\|F\|_{L_{\infty}\left(\supp\phi^{(n)}_{\nu}\right)}\leq\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\supp\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}}\|F\|_{L_{\infty}(I\times S)}
. Second, we apply
[
MSS26
, Lem. 3.5]
, in order to obtain

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L_{\infty}(I\times S)}\sim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2}}|I\times S|^{-\frac{1}{p}}\|F\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\sim_{\,d,\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{\frac{n}{p}\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)}\|F\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}.

Now the assertion follows using the (quasi-)norm equivalence in
\mathbb{R}^{\#\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}}
, which is finite dimensional with
\#\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}\lesssim_{\,d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}}1
according to
5.7
(iii)
.
∎

Lemma 5.9
.

Under the assumptions from
5.8
, it further holds

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})}|c_{\nu}|^{p}\,2^{-n\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\sim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|F\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}

Proof.

Rearranging the result from
5.8
and summing up over all
\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})
directly yields the asserted upper bound when we keep in mind that the supports of the basis functions have finite overlap depending only on
d
and
\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
. Conversely,

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}\sim_{\,d,p,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\left(\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})}|c_{\nu}|^{p}\|\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\sim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\left(\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})}|c_{\nu}|^{p}\,2^{-n\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

where we have used the (quasi-)norm equivalency for finite dimensional spaces together with the aforementioned finite overlap in the first step as well as
4.15
and
5.7
(iii)
in the second one.
∎

In the sequel, we employ the following (quasi-)norms on
\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
5.2
hold true. For
f\in\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
, we define

\displaystyle\|f\|^{E}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle:=|f|^{E}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}+\|f\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}:=\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}q}E\left(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}},\Omega_{T}\right)_{p}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}+\|f\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})},

\displaystyle\|f\|^{\pi}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle:=|f|^{\pi}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}+\|f\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}:=\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}q}\|f-\pi_{n}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}+\|f\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})},\text{ and}

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle:=\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})}|\supp\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}|^{-\frac{p}{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}+\frac{d}{\alpha_{2}}}}\left\|b_{\nu}^{(n)}(f)\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}\right\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{q}{p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

with the usual modifications for
q=\infty
, where the components in the last line stem from the multiscale decomposition given in
5.7
(ii)
. Then, the above expressions are all equivalent to
\|\cdot\|^{\Delta}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}
on
\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
with constants that depend (at most) on
d,\rho,p,q,s_{1},s_{2},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
, and
\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
, if
\alpha_{i}>0
,
i=1,2
. Further, if
\alpha_{i}\leq 0
,
i=1,2
, then at least
\|f\|^{\Delta}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}\sim\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim\|f\|^{E}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}\sim\|f\|^{\pi}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}
with the same dependency of the constants.

Remark 5.11
.

In particular, we stress that
\|\cdot\|^{E}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}
and due to the above lemma also
\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
do depend on
(r_{1},r_{2})
but not on the choice of
\rho
, at least if
\alpha_{i}>0
,
i=1,2
.

Proof.

We will only show the assertion for
q<\infty
. The case
q=\infty
works with the usual changes. First, we will show that
\|f\|^{\Delta}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}\sim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}
. Therefore, using the representation from
5.7
(ii)
and
5.9
, we obtain

\displaystyle 2^{n\frac{s_{2}}{d}q}\|\Delta_{n}(f)\|^{q}_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}\sim_{\,d,p,q,r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\left(\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})}2^{n\frac{s_{2}}{d}q}\,|b^{(n)}_{\nu}|^{p}\,2^{-n\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)}\right)^{\frac{q}{p}}.

The assertion now follows due to the fact that
2^{n\frac{s_{2}}{d}q}\sim_{\,d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|\supp\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}|^{-\frac{q}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}
for
\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})
, which is a consequence of the last result in
5.7
(iii)
together with
2^{-n\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)}\sim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}
due to
4.15
and
5.7
(iii)
.

Now we will estimate
\|f\|^{E}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}
,
\|f\|^{\pi}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}
, and
\|f\|^{\Delta}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}
against each other, where we proceed similar to the proof of
[
Lei00
, Thm. 3.3.3]
. First,
\|f\|^{E}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}\leq\|f\|^{\pi}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}
is a direct consequence of
\pi_{n}(f)\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}
. Second,
\|f\|^{\pi}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f\|^{E}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}
, due to
4.22
. Furthermore, following the calculations in
5.5
, in particular, (
27
), we obtain the estimate
\|f\|^{\Delta}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,p,q,\alpha_{2}}\|f\|^{\pi}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}
. Lastly, for
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\cup\{-1\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|f-\pi_{n}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{\min(p,1)}\leq\sum\limits_{j=n+1}^{\infty}\|\Delta_{j}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{\min(p,1)},

using the multiscale decomposition of
f
and the subadditivity of
\|\cdot\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{\min(p,1)}
. Now,
\|f\|^{\pi}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,p,q,\alpha_{2}}\|f\|^{\Delta}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}
, for
\alpha_{i}>0
,
i=1,2
, is the consequence of an application of the discrete Hardy’s inequality (cf. Lemma 3.4 of
[
DL93
, Ch. 2]
).
∎

Furthermore, we want to show that an embedding from the generalized anisotropic Besov spaces into the classical ones is also possible for small smoothness parameters. This can be proven by making good use of an appropriate multiscale decomposition of Besov functions, following the approach from
[
DP88
, Thm. 4.8]
.

Theorem 5.12
.

Let
5.2
hold,
0<\max(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})<1+\frac{1}{p}
, and
r_{i}>\alpha_{i}
,
i=1,2
. Then the embedding
\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})\hookrightarrow B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
holds true continuously with an embedding constant only depending on
d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
, and
\textup{LipProp}(\Omega)
.

Proof.

Let us initially fix the notation
p^{*}:=\min(p,1)
and choose
f\in\widehat{B}_{p,q}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(\Omega_{T})
. Further, for
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, let
f_{n},F_{n}\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}
such that

\displaystyle\|f-F_{n}\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}=E(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}},\Omega_{T})_{p}\quad\text{and}\quad f_{n}:=F_{n}-F_{n-1},

where
F_{-1}:=0
. We expand
f_{n}=\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})}c_{\nu}^{(n)}\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}
with respect to the basis
\left(\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}\right)_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})}
of
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}
. Now, choose
h\in\mathbb{R}
and
\bm{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with

\displaystyle|h|\leq r_{1}^{-1}\,2^{-\frac{n\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}}\left(\min\limits_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}|I|\right)\lesssim_{\,r_{1},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-\frac{n\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}}\quad\text{and}\quad|\bm{h}|\leq r_{2}^{-1}\,2^{-\frac{n}{d}}\left(\min\limits_{S\in\mathcal{T}_{0}}|S|\right)\lesssim_{\,d,r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-\frac{n}{d}},

respectively. Now we estimate

\displaystyle\|\Delta^{r_{1}}_{h,t}f\|_{L_{p}([0,T]_{r_{1},h}\times\Omega)}^{p^{*}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,r_{1}}\|f-F_{n}\|_{L_{p}([0,T\times\Omega))}^{p^{*}}+\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}\|\Delta^{r_{1}}_{h,t}f_{k}\|_{L_{p}([0,T]_{r_{1},h}\times\Omega)}^{p^{*}}

\displaystyle=E(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}},\Omega_{T})_{p}^{p^{*}}+\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}\|\Delta^{r_{1}}_{h,t}f_{k}\|_{L_{p}([0,T]_{r_{1},h}\times\Omega)}^{p^{*}},\quad n\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

(29)

using the identity
F_{n}=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}f_{k}
, the subadditivity of
\|\cdot\|_{L_{p}([0,T]_{r_{1},h}\times\Omega)}^{p^{*}}
, and the analogue of
2.1
(ii)
for differences. With the subadditivity, we also obtain

\displaystyle|\Delta_{h,t}^{r_{1}}f_{k}(t,\bm{x})|^{p^{*}}\leq\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{k})}|c_{\nu}^{(k)}|^{p^{*}}|\Delta_{h,t}^{r_{1}}\phi_{\nu}^{(k)}(t,\bm{x})|^{p^{*}}\lesssim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}}\left(\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{k})}|c_{\nu}^{(k)}|^{p}|\Delta_{h,t}^{r_{1}}\phi_{\nu}^{(k)}(t,\bm{x})|^{p}\right)^{\frac{p^{*}}{p}}

for every
k\in\{0,\dots,n\}
and
(t,\bm{x})\in[0,T]_{r_{1},h}\times\Omega
, by using the expansion of
f_{k}
in the basis of
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}
. Additionally, in the second step, the (quasi-)norm equivalency in finite dimensional spaces has been used together with the fact that the basis functions have finite overlap depending only on
d
and
\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
. Furthermore, we can estimate

\displaystyle|\Delta_{h,t}^{r_{1}}f_{k}(t,\bm{x})|^{p}\lesssim_{\,d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|h|^{p}\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{k})}|c_{\nu}^{(k)}|^{p}\,\textup{diam}_{t}\left(\supp\phi^{(k)}_{\nu}\right)^{-p}\cdot\mathds{1}_{\supp\left(\Delta^{r_{1}}_{h,t}\phi_{\nu}^{(k)}\right)}(t,\bm{x})

(30)

using the temporal part of (
22
) as well as the fact that we have chosen
h
to fulfill
|h|\leq\textup{diam}_{t}\left(\supp\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}\right)\leq\textup{diam}_{t}\left(\supp\phi_{\nu}^{(k)}\right)
. Employing the estimate for
\textup{diam}_{t}\left(\supp\phi^{(k)}_{\nu}\right)
from
5.7
(iv)
, we now integrate over
[0,T]_{r_{1},h}\times\Omega
with respect to
(t,\bm{x})
in order to derive

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{h,t}^{r_{1}}f_{k}\|^{p}_{L_{p}([0,T]_{r_{1},h}\times\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|h|^{p}\,2^{\frac{k\alpha_{2}p}{\alpha_{1}d}}\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{k})}|c_{\nu}^{(k)}|^{p}\,\left|\supp\left(\Delta_{h,t}^{r_{1}}\phi_{\nu}^{(k)}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,r_{1},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|h|^{1+p}\,2^{\frac{k\alpha_{2}p}{\alpha_{1}d}}\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{k})}|c_{\nu}^{(k)}|^{p}\,\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}\left(\supp\phi_{\nu}^{(k)}\right)^{d}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|h|^{1+p}\,2^{\frac{k\alpha_{2}p}{\alpha_{1}d}(1+p)}\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{k})}|c_{\nu}^{(k)}|^{p}\,2^{-k\left(1+\frac{\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}\right)}.

Here, we have additionally used the left part of (
23
) together with (
24
) in the second step as well as the estimate for
\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}\left(\supp\phi^{(k)}_{\nu}\right)
from
5.7
(iv)
in the last one. Using, the result from
5.9
, this becomes

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{h,t}^{r_{1}}f_{k}\|^{p}_{L_{p}([0,T]_{r_{1},h}\times\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|h|^{1+p}\,2^{\frac{k\alpha_{2}p}{\alpha_{1}d}(1+p)}\|f_{k}\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}

(31)

\displaystyle=|h|^{1+p}\,2^{\frac{k\alpha_{2}p}{\alpha_{1}d}(1+p)}\left(E(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{k}},\Omega_{T})_{p}^{p}+E(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{k-1}},\Omega_{T})_{p}^{p}\right),

where we use
E(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{-1}},\Omega_{T})_{p}:=\|f\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}
. Inserting (
31
) into (
5.1
), taking the supremum over all considered
h
and applying the estimate from
2.1
(iii)
, yields

\displaystyle\omega_{r_{1},t}\left(f,\Omega_{T},2^{-\frac{n\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}}\right)_{p}^{p^{*}}\lesssim_{\,d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-\frac{n\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)p^{*}}\sum\limits_{k=-1}^{n}2^{\frac{k\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)p^{*}}E(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{k}},\Omega_{T})_{p}^{p^{*}}.

Since
0<\alpha_{1}<1+\frac{1}{p}
,
\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}<\frac{\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)
, the discrete Hardy inequality yields

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}q}\omega_{r_{1},t}\left(f,\Omega_{T},2^{-\frac{n\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}}\right)_{p}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim_{\,d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f\|^{E}_{\widehat{B}_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}^{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}.

(32)

Here, we have used the version of the inequality, which one can derive from
[
DL93
, Lem. 3.4 of Ch. 2]
, considering what is pointed out in the corresponding remark, in particular, Eq. (3.13). The spatial part works similar. We obtain

\displaystyle|\Delta_{\bm{h},\bm{x}}^{r_{2}}f_{k}(t,\bm{x})|^{p}\lesssim_{\,d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|\bm{h}|^{p}\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{k})}|c_{\nu}^{(k)}|^{p}\,\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}\left(\supp\phi^{(k)}_{\nu}\right)^{-p}\cdot\mathds{1}_{\supp\left(\Delta^{r_{2}}_{\bm{h},\bm{x}}\phi_{\nu}^{(k)}\right)}(t,\bm{x})

on
[0,T]\times\Omega_{r_{2},h}
, correspondingly to the derivation of (
30
). This then leads to

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{\bm{h},\bm{x}}^{r_{2}}f_{k}\|^{p}_{L_{p}([0,T]\times\Omega_{r_{2},h})}\lesssim_{\,\begin{subarray}{c}d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\\
\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})\end{subarray}}|\bm{h}|^{1+p}\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{k})}|c_{\nu}^{(k)}|^{p}\,\textup{diam}_{t}\left(\supp\phi^{(k)}_{\nu}\right)\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}\left(\supp\phi^{(k)}_{\nu}\right)^{d-1-p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|\bm{h}|^{1+p}\,2^{\frac{k}{d}(1+p)}\sum\limits_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{k})}|c_{\nu}^{(k)}|^{p}\,2^{-k\left(1+\frac{\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}\right)}

using the right part of (
23
) together with (
25
) and
|\bm{h}|\leq\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}\left(\supp\phi_{\nu}^{(k)}\right)
in the first step as well as
5.7
(iv)
in the second one. Further, combining the temporal analogues of (
5.1
) and (
31
) as well as going over to the supremum and using the scaling property of the moduli of smoothness, yields

\displaystyle\omega_{r_{2},\bm{x}}\left(f,\Omega_{T},2^{-\frac{n}{d}}\right)_{p}^{p^{*}}\lesssim_{\,d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-\frac{n}{d}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)p^{*}}\sum\limits_{k=-1}^{n}2^{\frac{k}{d}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)p^{*}}E(f,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{k}},\Omega_{T})_{p}^{p^{*}},

which implies

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}q}\omega_{r_{2},\bm{x}}\left(f,\Omega_{T},2^{-\frac{n}{d}}\right)_{p}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim_{\,d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f\|^{E}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}

(33)

by Hardy’s inequality, where we have exploited that
\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}<\frac{1}{d}\left(1+\frac{1}{p}\right)
, since
\alpha_{2}<1+\frac{1}{p}
. Adding (
32
), (
33
) and
\|f\|_{L_{p}(\Omega)}
now yields
\|f\|^{*,(r_{1},r_{2})}_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f\|^{E}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})}
. With the (quasi-)norm equivalency result from the end of
Section

2.1.2
, the assertion finally follows, since
r_{i}>\alpha_{i}
,
i=1,2
.
∎

Corollary 5.13
.

Under
5.2
,
B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})=\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
in the sense of equivalent (quasi-)norms for
r_{i}>\alpha_{i}>0
,
i=1,2
, if and only if
\alpha_{i}<1+\frac{1}{p}
,
i=1,2
, where the embedding constant only depends on
d,\rho,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
, and
\textup{LipProp}(\Omega)
. In particular, this implies that all the spaces
\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
coincide for different choices of the polynomial degrees as long as
r_{i}>\alpha_{i}
,
i=1,2
.

Proof.

That the result holds true for
\max(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})<1+\frac{1}{p}
is a consequence of
5.5
together with
5.12
. In turn, for any space-time partition
\mathcal{P}
created from
\mathcal{P}_{0}
by finitely many applications of
\textup{PATCH}\_\textup{REFINE}(\cdot,\cdot,d,s_{1},s_{2})
,
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}\subsetneq B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
and
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}\subset\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,q}(\Omega_{T})
due to
5.1
and
5.7
(i)
, correspondingly, yielding it for
\max(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\geq 1+\frac{1}{p}
.
∎

This corollary allows us to show a Sobolev-type embedding between anisotropic Besov spaces of different smoothness, which can be seen as an extension of
[
MSS26
, Thm. 1.3]
.

Theorem 5.14
.

Let
5.2
hold,
p,q\in(0,\infty]
,
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
with
\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}>0
, and
0<\alpha_{i}<1+\frac{1}{p}
,
i=1,2
. Then, the embedding
B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})\hookrightarrow B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})
is continuous with a constant depending only on
d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0})
, and
\textup{LipProp}(\Omega)
.

Remark 5.15
.

We point out that the restriction on
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
is purely due to the fact that the anisotropic Besov regularity of our approximants does not become larger for increasing polynomial degrees. If smoother approximants are available, where the latter is the case for example for anisotropic splines on simpler domains like rectangular ones, it should be possible to show the above theorem without the restriction on the smoothness parameters
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
.

Proof.

First, we will consider the case
q\geq p
. Then, the embeddings
B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})\hookrightarrow B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{q,p}(\Omega_{T})\hookrightarrow B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})
are continuous due to
[
MSS26
, Rem. 3.2(ii) and (iii)]
. Now we can consider the opposite case, i.e.,
q<p
. We will only present the proof for
p<\infty
, the other case works similarly with the usual changes. Now let
f\in B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})
and put
r_{i}:=\max(\lfloor\alpha_{i}+s_{i}\rfloor,2)
,
i=1,2
. Now setting
\rho:=q
and employing
5.13
yields
\|f\|_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),\textup{LipProp}(\Omega)}\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}^{\pi}
, so it will suffice to bound
\|f\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}
and
|f|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}^{\pi}
. Since
\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}>0
, also
\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}+s_{1}}+\frac{d}{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}>0
, and therefore
B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})\hookrightarrow L_{p}(\Omega_{T})
, i.e.,
\|f\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f\|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}
according to
[
MSS26
, Thm. 1.3]
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle|f|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}^{\pi}=\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}p}\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\|f-\pi_{n}(f)\|^{p}_{L_{p}(I\times S)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\,d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\\
\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0})\end{subarray}}\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}p}\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}|I\times S|^{\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}+s_{1}}+\frac{d}{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}\right)p}|f|^{p}_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}\left(\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I\times S)\right)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}p}\left(\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}|I\times S|^{\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}+s_{1}}+\frac{d}{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}\right)q}|f|^{q}_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}\left(\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I\times S)\right)}\right)^{\frac{p}{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}p}\,2^{-n\left(1+\frac{\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}+s_{1}}+\frac{d}{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}\right)p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(2^{-\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}\right)p}\right)^{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}\sim_{\,d,p,q,s_{1},s_{2}}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}

by
4.21
and
4.23
in the second step
13
13

13

Note, that
a(\mathcal{P}_{0})=a(\mathcal{P}_{0},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})=a(\mathcal{P}_{0},\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2})
due to the collinearity of
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})
and
(s_{1},s_{2})
.
, the embedding
\ell^{q}(\mathbb{N}_{0})\hookrightarrow\ell^{p}(\mathbb{N}_{0})
, due to
q<p
,
in the third one as well as the finite overlap due to
5.7
(iii)
together with
[
MSS26
, Cor. 3.8]
in the fourth. The penultimate step is the result of a lengthy algebraic calculation exploiting the linear dependency of
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})
and
(s_{1},s_{2})
, provided in
Appendix

A
, whereas the last one is due to the fact that the geometric sum converges, since
\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}>0
. Thus, the assertion is shown.
∎

5.2
Direct estimates

This section will now be devoted to the proof of
1
. In order to do so, will will first have to localize the (quasi-)norm in
B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})
. This corresponds to the result from
[
GM14
, Prop. 2.1, Eq. (3) and (4)]
, whereas our proof is a little shorter due to the machinery shown in the previous section.

Lemma 5.16
.

We assume
5.2
to hold true with
0<\alpha_{i}<\min\left(1+\frac{1}{p},r_{i}\right)
,
i=1,2
, and
\mathcal{P}
to be obtained from
\mathcal{P}_{0}
by finitely many applications of
\textup{PATCH}\_\textup{REFINE}(\cdot,\cdot,d,s_{1},s_{2})
. Then,

\displaystyle\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0}),\textup{LipProp}(\Omega)}\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}}\left(|f|^{(r_{1},r_{2}),*}_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}\left(\widetilde{\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}}(I\times S)\right)}\right)^{p}

for
p<\infty
with the classical modification for
p=\infty
.

Proof.

We will only show the case of
p<\infty
and employ the result of
5.12
,

\displaystyle\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}\lesssim_{\,\begin{subarray}{c}d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},\\
r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),\\
\textup{LipProp}(\Omega)\end{subarray}}\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}p}E(f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f),\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}},\Omega_{T})_{p}^{p}\right)+\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}.

(34)

We already know from
4.21
together with
4.23
that

\displaystyle\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}}\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,\begin{subarray}{c}d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\\
\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0})\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}}|I\times S|^{\frac{p}{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}+\frac{d}{\alpha_{2}}}}\left(|f|^{(r_{1},r_{2}),*}_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}\left(\widetilde{\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}}(I\times S)\right)}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,\begin{subarray}{c}d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}}\left(|f|^{(r_{1},r_{2}),*}_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}\left(\widetilde{\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}}(I\times S)\right)}\right)^{p}.

(35)

Now we have to estimate the sum in (
34
). Therefore,

\displaystyle E(f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f),\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}},\Omega_{T})_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{I_{n}\times S_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)-\pi_{n}(f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f))\|_{L_{p}(I_{n}\times S_{n})}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\sum\limits_{I_{n}\times S_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}E\left(f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f),\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I_{n}\times S_{n})\right)_{p}^{p}

according to
4.21
. Now we have to distinguish two cases,
\mathcal{P}_{n}=\mathcal{P}_{n}^{(1)}\overset{\circ}{\cup}\mathcal{P}_{n}^{(2)}
, where
\mathcal{P}_{n}^{(1)}:=\{I_{n}\times S_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}\mid\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I_{n}\times S_{n})\subset I\times S,\>\>I\times S\in\mathcal{P}\}
and
\mathcal{P}_{n}^{(2)}:=\mathcal{P}_{n}\setminus\mathcal{P}_{n}^{(1)}
. The first case means, that
\mathcal{P}_{n}
is locally so much finer than
\mathcal{P}
that
\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)_{|\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I_{n}\times S_{n})}
is a polynomial. Then, we can estimate

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{I_{n}\times S_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}^{(1)}}E\left(f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f),\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I_{n}\times S_{n})\right)_{p}^{p}=\sum\limits_{I_{n}\times S_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}^{(1)}}E\left(f,\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I_{n}\times S_{n})\right)_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\sum\limits_{I_{n}\times S_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}^{(1)}}\omega_{r_{1},t}\left(f,\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I_{n}\times S_{n}),2^{-\frac{n\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}}\right)_{p}^{p}+\omega_{r_{2},\bm{x}}\left(f,\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I_{n}\times S_{n}),2^{-\frac{n}{d}}\right)_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}}\omega_{r_{1},t}\left(f,I\times S,2^{-\frac{n\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}}\right)_{p}^{p}+\omega_{r_{2},\bm{x}}\left(f,I\times S,2^{-\frac{n}{d}}\right)_{p}^{p},

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}}\omega_{r_{1},t}\left(f,\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S),2^{-\frac{n\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}}\right)_{p}^{p}+\omega_{r_{2},\bm{x}}\left(f,\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S),2^{-\frac{n}{d}}\right)_{p}^{p},

(36)

using Jackson’s estimate from
[
MSS26
, Thm. 1.1]
and then proceeding similar to before in the proof of
5.5
, in particular, equations (
27
) and (
5.1
). Otherwise, if
I_{n}\times S_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}^{(2)}
, then all the elements of
\mathcal{P}
which have non-empty intersection with
\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I_{n}\times S_{n})
have to have comparable size, i.e., for
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}(I_{n}\times S_{n}):=\{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}\mid(I\times S)\cap\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I_{n}\times S_{n})\neq\emptyset\}
,
\textup{diam}_{t}(I\times S)\lesssim_{\,d,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-\frac{n\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}}}
and
\textup{diam}_{\bm{x}}(I\times S)\lesssim_{\,d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-\frac{n}{d}}
. Let
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}:=\bigcup\limits_{I_{n}\times S_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\mathcal{P}(I_{n}\times S_{n})
, then, due to the finite overlap of neighborhood domains from
5.7
(iii)
, we can estimate

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{I_{n}\times S_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}^{(2)}}E\left(f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f),\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I_{n}\times S_{n})\right)_{p}^{p}\leq\sum\limits_{I_{n}\times S_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}^{(2)}}\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}\left(\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}^{j(d)}(I_{n}\times S_{n})\right)}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}\left(I\times S\right)}^{p}\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}E\left(f,\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}},\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S)\right)_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}}\omega_{r_{1},t}\left(f,\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S),2^{-\frac{n\alpha_{2}}{\alpha_{1}d}}\right)_{p}^{p}+\omega_{r_{2},\bm{x}}\left(f,\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S),2^{-\frac{n}{d}}\right)_{p}^{p},

(37)

where we have additionally employed
4.21
in the third step and proceeded like before in (
5.2
), while using the scaling properties of the moduli of smoothness together with their monotonicity and the aforementioned bound on the temporal and spacial diameter in the last one. Lastly, adding (
5.2
) to (
5.2
) yields the necessary bound on the sum from (
5.2
).
∎

We can further estimate the local Besov (quasi-)seminorm of lower order with one of higher order. This result corresponds to
[
GM14
, Lem. 4.17]
in the stationary setting.

Lemma 5.17
.

Under the assumptions of
5.16
, while additionally requiring
\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}>0
as well as
r_{i}>\alpha_{i}+s_{i}
,
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle|f|^{(r_{1},r_{2}),*}_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}\left(\widetilde{\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}}(I\times S)\right)}\lesssim_{\,\begin{subarray}{c}d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},\\
r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0})\end{subarray}}|I\times S|^{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}}|f|_{B_{q,q}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}\left(\widetilde{\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}}(I\times S)\right)}

holds true for any
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}
.

Proof.

We employ the notation from
4.23
, i.e.,
J\times R:=\widetilde{\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}}(I\times S)
,
R_{ref}
as the corresponding spacial reference domain, and
\tilde{f}
as the rescaled version of
f
on
[0,1]\times R_{ref}
. Due to (
4.2
) together with (
17
) (and their analogue for the discrete (quasi-)seminorm), we have

\displaystyle|\tilde{f}|_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}([0,1]\times R_{ref})}\sim_{\,d,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|J\times R|^{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}+\frac{d}{\alpha_{2}}}-\frac{1}{p}}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(J\times R)}

as well as

\displaystyle|\tilde{f}|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R_{ref})}\sim_{\,d,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|J\times R|^{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}+s_{1}}+\frac{d}{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(J\times R)}.

Here, we keep in mind that
a(\mathcal{P}_{0})=a(\mathcal{P}_{0},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})=a(\mathcal{P}_{0},\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2})
due to the linear dependency of
(s_{1},s_{2})
and
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})
. The latter now additionally implies that it suffices to show the result on
[0,1]\times R_{ref}
with respect to
\tilde{f}
. So, we set
\tilde{P}:=\mathcal{B}_{q,[0,1]\times R_{ref}}(\tilde{f})
with respect to
(r_{1},r_{2})
. Then,

\displaystyle|\tilde{f}

\displaystyle|^{(r_{1},r_{2}),*}_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}([0,1]\times R_{ref})}=|\tilde{f}-\tilde{P}|^{(r_{1},r_{2}),*}_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}([0,1]\times R_{ref})}\lesssim_{\,\begin{subarray}{c}d,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\\
\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})\end{subarray}}\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{P}\|_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}([0,1]\times R_{ref})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,\begin{subarray}{c}d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\\
\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0})\end{subarray}}\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{P}\|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R_{ref})}\lesssim_{\,\begin{subarray}{c}d,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2}\\
r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})\end{subarray}}\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{P}\|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R_{ref})}^{(r_{1},r_{2})}

\displaystyle=\|\tilde{f}-\tilde{P}\|_{L_{q}([0,1]\times R_{ref})}+|\tilde{f}|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R_{ref})}^{(r_{1},r_{2})}\lesssim_{\,\begin{subarray}{c}d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},\\
r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0})\end{subarray}}|\tilde{f}|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}([0,1]\times R_{ref})}.

We have applied the result of
[
MSS26
, Lem. 2.16]
in the first and penultimate step as well as the (quasi-)norm equivalency for anisotropic Besov spaces mentioned at the end of
Section

2.1.2
with
\textup{LipProp}(R_{ref})\sim_{\,d,\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}1
in the second and fourth step. Additionally, in the third one, we have used the embedding result from
5.14
as well as
4.23
and
2.1
(ii)
in the last one. This shows the assertion.
∎

Let us now consider the algorithm
\textup{MARKED}\_\textup{REFINE}(\mathcal{P}_{0},d,s_{1},s_{2})
from
Section

3.3
with respect to the marking routine

\displaystyle\textup{MARK}(\mathcal{P}):=\left\{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}\mid|I\times S|^{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}\left(\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S)\right)}>\delta\right\}

for any corresponding space-time partition
\mathcal{P}
of
\Omega_{T}
. The resulting algorithm is a type of greedy algorithm and is very similar to corresponding ones in the literature, for example in
[
BDDP02
,
GM14
,
AMS23
,
MSS26
]
.

As in
Section

3.3
, we will denote the marked elements in the space-time partition at the end of the
k
-th loop of the
while
-clause of the routine by
\mathcal{M}_{k}
, for
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
5.2
hold with
\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}>0
as well as
0<\alpha_{i}<1+\frac{1}{p}
,
i=1,2
. The algorithm
\textup{MARKED}\_\textup{REFINE}(\mathcal{P}_{0},d,s_{1},s_{2})
with respect to the above marking method terminates for every
\delta\in(0,\infty)
and
f\in B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the one of
[
MSS26
, Lem. 4.3]
. If the algorithm did not terminate, there would be
I_{k}\times S_{k}\in\mathcal{M}_{k}
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
. For those prisms, we know that
|I_{k}\times S_{k}|\xrightarrow{k\rightarrow\infty}0
, since the atomic refinement method gets called to
I_{k}\times S_{k}
during the procedure, which at least halves the volume of prisms to which it is called. Since
I_{k}\times S_{k}\in\mathcal{M}_{k}
, we can therefore estimate

\displaystyle\delta<|I_{k}\times S_{k}|^{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}\xrightarrow{k\rightarrow\infty}0,

which is a contradiction to
\delta>0
.
∎

By carefully choosing an appropriate
\delta
, we can proceed to the following theorem, which will be the first part of the almost characterization. This results together with the embedding from
[
MSS26
, Thm. 1.3]
and
5.14
, respectively, shows
1
. In it, we will exploit the notation
B^{0,0}_{p,p}:=L_{p}
for simplicity.

A version of this theorem for the adaptive approximation with discontinuous, anisotropic polynomials was already proven in
[
MSS26
, Thm. 1.5]
.

Theorem 5.19
.

Let
p,q,s_{1},s_{2},f
as in
5.18
,
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}(s_{1},s_{2})
with
\alpha_{i}<1+\frac{1}{p}
, and
\rho\in(0,\min(p,q)]
. Further, consider
r_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
with
r_{i}>\alpha_{i}+s_{i}
,
i=1,2
, and
\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}
with respect to
(r_{1},r_{2})
. Additionally, choose an arbitrary
\varepsilon>0
. Then, there is
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon,d,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},p,f)>0
such that
\textup{MARKED}\_\textup{REFINE}(\mathcal{P}_{0},d,s_{1},s_{2})
with the above marking technique terminates with a space-time partition
\mathcal{P}
which satisfies

\displaystyle\#\mathcal{P}-\#\mathcal{P}_{0}\lesssim_{\,d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),|\Omega_{T}|}\varepsilon^{-\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}\right)}\quad

(38)

and

\displaystyle\quad\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0}),\textup{LipProp}(\Omega),\#\mathcal{P}_{0}}\varepsilon|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}.

(39)

Proof.

Again, we assume
r_{i}:=\max(\lfloor\alpha_{i}+s_{i}\rfloor+1,2)
,
i=1,2
, without loss of generality. If
|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}=0
, then
f\in\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}(\Omega_{T})
due to
[
MSS26
, Lem. 2.16]
and
4.21
together with
4.23
implies
\mathcal{M}_{0}=\emptyset
. Thus, the algorithm terminates with
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}_{0}
for arbitrary
\delta>0
.
\mathcal{P}_{0}
of course, fulfills the first assertion. Since
f\in\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}
, we have
\mathcal{B}_{p,\mathcal{P}}(f)=f
because
f
itself is the best approximation of
f
on any domain with respect to elements of
\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}(\Omega_{T})
. Furthermore,
4.14
implies
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}(f)=f
, i.e.,
\pi_{p,\mathcal{P}}(f)=f
and therefore the second assertion is true.

Now let us consider the case
|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}>0
and set
\delta:=\varepsilon^{1+\frac{1}{s_{1}p}+\frac{d}{s_{2}p}}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}>0
. According to
5.18
, the algorithm terminates with
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}_{k}
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{M}_{j}\neq\emptyset
if and only if
j\leq k-1
. We define

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{M}}:=\bigcup\limits_{j=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{M}_{j}\quad\text{and}\quad\Gamma_{j}:=\{I\times S\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}\mid\ell(I\times S)=j\}\quad\text{for}\quad j\in\mathbb{N}.

Notice that
3.10
implies that also
\Gamma_{j}=\emptyset
for
j\geq k
. Our goal will be to bound the cardinality of
\Gamma_{j}
appropriately. On the one hand, for every
I\times S\in\Gamma_{j}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
|I\times S|\sim_{\,\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-j\left(\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}+1\right)}
and therefore
\#\Gamma_{j}\lesssim_{\,\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),|\Omega_{T}|}2^{j\left(\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}+1\right)}
. On the other hand, consider the following calculation

\displaystyle\#\Gamma_{j}\delta^{q}=\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\Gamma_{j}}\delta^{q}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\Gamma_{j}}|I\times S|^{\frac{q}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-1+\frac{q}{p}}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S))}^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,q,\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),|\Omega_{T}|}2^{-jq\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}\right)}\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\Gamma_{j}}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S))}^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-jq\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}\right)}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{q},

where we have used that
\Gamma_{j}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}
in the second step and
2^{-j\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)}\sim_{\,\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),|\Omega_{T}|}|I\times S|
for
I\times S\in\Gamma_{j}
as well as
\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}>0
in the third one. In the last step, the result from
[
MSS26
, Cor. 3.8]
has been applied together with the finite overlap of the extended neighborhoods, due to

\displaystyle 2^{-j\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)}\sim_{\,\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),|\Omega_{T}|}|I\times S|\sim_{\,d,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|\widetilde{\omega^{j(d)}_{\mathcal{P}}}(I\times S)|

according to
3.13
(ii)
. Thus,

\displaystyle\#\Gamma_{j}\lesssim_{\,d,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),|\Omega_{T}|}\min\left(2^{j\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)},\frac{1}{\delta^{q}}2^{-jq\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}\right)}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{q}\right).

Now the identical calculations to those from
[
MSS26
, Eq. (57)–(60)]
show the asserted estimate in (
38
) for
\#\overline{\mathcal{M}}
instead of
\#\mathcal{P}-\#\mathcal{P}_{0}
, for
q<\infty
. The desired result itself is now a consequence of the complexity result from
3.15
. The case
q=\infty
can be achieved similarly with the usual modifications. Now we show the second assertion for
p<\infty
, the case
p=\infty
works, again, correspondingly. Let
C
be constant from (
38
). First, we consider the case, where
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}>C
, in particular,
\varepsilon>1
due to
C\geq 1
. Then, (
38
) implies that
\#\mathcal{P}-\#\mathcal{P}_{0}=0
, i.e.,
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}_{0}
. Therefore, for
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\neq(0,0)
,

\displaystyle\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}_{0}}(f)\|_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0}),\textup{LipProp}(\Omega)}\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{0}}\left(|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}\left(\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S)\right)}^{(r_{1},r_{2}),*}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,\begin{subarray}{c}d,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0})\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{0}}|I\times S|^{p\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}\right)}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S))}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}\leq\varepsilon^{p}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{p},

due to
5.16
in the first step,
5.17
in the second,
[
MSS26
, Cor. 3.8]
as before above in the third one, and
\varepsilon>1
in the last one. Now we can turn to the case, where
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}\leq C
. Then, similarly

\displaystyle\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,\begin{subarray}{c}d,\rho,p,q,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\\
\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),a(\mathcal{P}_{0}),\textup{LipProp}(\Omega)\end{subarray}}\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{0}}|I\times S|^{p\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}\right)}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\widetilde{\omega_{\mathcal{P}}^{j(d)}}(I\times S))}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\#\mathcal{P}\,\delta^{p}\leq\left(\#\mathcal{P}_{0}+C\varepsilon^{-\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}\right)}\right)\delta^{p}\leq C(\#\mathcal{P}_{0}+1)\varepsilon^{-\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}\right)}\delta^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,\#\mathcal{P}_{0}}\varepsilon^{-\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}\right)}\varepsilon^{p+\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}=\varepsilon^{p}|f|_{B^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{p},

where, the fact that the algorithm has terminated with
\mathcal{P}
has been additionally applied in the second step, as well as (
38
) in the third,
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}\leq C
in the fourth, and the definition of
\delta
in the penultimate one. For
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})=(0,0)
, we can obtain corresponding results, using
\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}=\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}}\|f-\pi_{\rho,\mathcal{P}}(f)\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}
,
4.21
and
4.23
instead of
5.16
and
5.17
.
∎

Discussion 5.20
.

It is noteworthy, that for
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})=(0,0)
, this result for continuous finite elements yields the same qualitative dependency on the parameters as the result for the discontinuous case from
[
MSS26
, Thm. 1.5]
. Therefore, from an approximation theoretical viewpoint, there is no significant advantage in using discontinuous anisotropic space-time finite elements over continuous ones. This result is consistent to the findings in
[
Vee16
]
, where it was shown that for the approximation of stationary functions with respect to the (broken) Sobolev-norm on a conforming triangulation, there is no qualitative advantage to using discontinuous FEM over continuous ones.

5.3
Inverse estimates

The goal of this section is to show inverse estimates, i.e.,
2
. From now on we will always work under the following assumptions on the parameters.

Assumption 5.21
.

Let
5.2
hold with
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}=\frac{1}{q}
, which, in particular, implies
q<p
. Additionally, we require
\rho\leq q
and use the notation
q^{*}:=\min(1,q)
. Further,
\mathcal{P}
will always represent a space-time partition created from
\mathcal{P}_{0}
by finitely many applications of
\textup{PATCH}\_\textup{REFINE}(\cdot,\cdot,d,s_{1},s_{2})
. We will denote
\mathcal{P}\in\,\mathbb{P}_{N}:=\mathbb{P}_{N}(\mathcal{P}_{0})
, if at most
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
applications of this method were used to create
\mathcal{P}
from
\mathcal{P}_{0}
.

Remark 5.22
.

If
\mathcal{P}\in\mathbb{P}_{N}
, then
\#\mathcal{P}-\#\mathcal{P}_{0}\sim_{\,d,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}N
, due to
3.15
.

For anisotropic finite elements on
\mathcal{P}\in\mathbb{P}_{N}
, we can estimate higher order generalized (anisotropic) Besov
(quasi-)
norms appropriately by those of lesser order, as can be seen in the following lemma. It corresponds to
[
GM14
, Thm. 7.3]
(in the stationary case) and is an estimate of Bernstein type.

Lemma 5.23

(Bernstein-type inequality)
.

Let
5.21
hold true. Then, for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{P}\in\mathbb{P}_{N}
, and
F\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
, we can estimate

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),\#\mathcal{P}_{0}}N^{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\|F\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}.

Proof.

Consider the multiscale approximation of
F
from
5.7
(ii)
and define

\displaystyle\Lambda:=\{(n,\nu)\mid n\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}_{n})\text{ with }b_{\nu}^{(n)}\neq 0\},~i.e.,~F=\sum\limits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}b_{\lambda}(F)\phi_{\lambda},

where
b_{\lambda}(F)=b_{\nu}^{(n)}(F)
and
\phi_{\lambda}=\phi_{\nu}^{(n)}
for
\lambda=(n,\nu)\in\Lambda
. Further, consider the tree
\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{P}}
of all the prisms which have been created during the sequence of applications of the
\textup{PATCH}\_\textup{REFINE}(\cdot,\cdot,d,s_{1},s_{2})
procedure corresponding to
\mathcal{P}
. Then, surely
\Lambda\subset\bigcup\limits_{I\times S\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{P}}}\left\{(\ell(I\times S),\nu^{\prime})\mid\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(I\times S)\right\}
, which yields
\#\Lambda\lesssim_{\,d,r_{1},r_{2}}\#\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{P}}
. Since every application of
\textup{PATCH}\_\textup{REFINE}(\cdot,\cdot,d,s_{1},s_{2})
produces at most
\sim_{\,d,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\,2^{\left\lceil 1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right\rceil}
new elements, we obtain

\displaystyle\#\Lambda\lesssim_{\,d,r_{1},r_{2}}\#\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\lesssim_{\,d,s_{1},s_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\#\mathcal{P}_{0}+N\lesssim_{\,\#\mathcal{P}_{0}}N.

(40)

Now, we use Hölder’s inequality with respect to sums and integrals, respectively, to obtain

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum\limits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}|\supp\phi_{\lambda}|^{-\frac{q}{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}+s_{1}}+\frac{d}{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}}}\left\|b_{\lambda}(f)\phi_{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{q}(\Omega_{T})}^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq(\#\Lambda)^{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\left(\sum\limits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}|\supp\phi_{\lambda}|^{-\frac{p}{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}+s_{1}}+\frac{d}{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}}}\left\|b_{\lambda}(f)\phi_{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{q}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq(\#\Lambda)^{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\left(\sum\limits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}|\supp\phi_{\lambda}|^{-\frac{p}{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}+s_{1}}+\frac{d}{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}}}|\supp\phi_{\lambda}|^{\frac{p}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\left\|b_{\lambda}(f)\phi_{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=(\#\Lambda)^{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\left(\sum\limits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}|\supp\phi_{\lambda}|^{-\frac{p}{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}+\frac{d}{\alpha_{2}}}}\left\|b_{\lambda}(f)\phi_{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),\#\mathcal{P}_{0}}N^{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\left(\sum\limits_{\lambda\in\Lambda}|\supp\phi_{\lambda}|^{-\frac{p}{\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}+\frac{d}{\alpha_{2}}}}\left\|b_{\lambda}(f)\phi_{\lambda}\right\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=N^{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})},

where we have used the linear dependency of
(\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2})
and
(s_{1},s_{2})
in the fourth step, as well as (
40
) in the penultimate one.
∎

Further, we need a similar result for the Lebesgue norm of the finite elements on the partitions of the multiscale decomposition
\mathcal{P}_{n}
that corresponds to the one given in
[
DP88
, Ep. (7.2)]
.

Lemma 5.24
.

Let
5.21
hold and consider
F\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{n\frac{s_{2}}{d}}\|F\|_{L_{q}(\Omega_{T})}

Proof.

Assume
p<\infty
, the case
p=\infty
is very similar. Using
[
MSS26
, Lem. 3.5]
,
|I\times S|\sim_{\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-n\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)}
, the identity for
q,p,s_{1}
, and
s_{2}
, and the embedding
\ell^{\frac{q}{p}}(\mathbb{N}_{0})\hookrightarrow\ell^{1}(\mathbb{N}_{0})
due to
q<p
, yields

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\|F\|_{L_{p}(I\times S)}^{p}\sim_{\,d,p,r_{1},r_{2}}\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}|I\times S|^{1-\frac{p}{q}}\|F\|_{L_{q}(I\times S)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,s_{1},s_{2},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}2^{-n\left(1+\frac{s_{2}}{s_{1}d}\right)\left(-\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}\right)p}\left(\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\|F\|_{L_{q}(I\times S)}^{q}\right)^{\frac{p}{q}}=2^{n\frac{s_{2}}{d}p}\|F\|_{L_{q}(\Omega_{T})}^{p}.\qed

In particular, this result allows us to show embeddings between generalized anisotropic Besov spaces of different smoothness, similar to the results from
[
MSS26
, Thm. 1.3]
and
5.14
for the classical anisotropic spaces.

Lemma 5.25
.

The embedding
\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})\hookrightarrow\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})
is continuous with an embedding constant only depending on
d,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2}
, and
\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
.

Proof.

We estimate

\displaystyle\|f\|^{\Delta}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}p}\|\Delta_{n}(f)\|^{p}_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim_{\,d,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n\frac{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}{d}p}\|\Delta_{n}(f)\|^{p}_{L_{q}(\Omega_{T})}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n\frac{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}{d}q}\|\Delta_{n}(f)\|^{q}_{L_{q}(\Omega_{T})}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}=\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{\Delta}

using
5.24
and the embedding
\ell^{q}(\mathbb{N}_{0})\hookrightarrow\ell^{p}(\mathbb{N}_{0})
due to
q<p
.
∎

The results that we show in the following are closely related to sequence spaces and (their) real interpolation.
We now introduce the corresponding definitions and results for the sake of completeness.

Definition 5.26
.

For
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
,
\eta\in(0,\infty)
, and a (quasi-)Banach space
X
, we define the
generalized sequence spaces
via

\displaystyle\ell^{\gamma,\eta}(X):=\bigg\{a:\mathbb{N}_{0}\rightarrow X\ \bigg|\ \|a\|_{\ell^{\gamma,\eta}(X)}:=\Big(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(2^{\gamma k}\|a_{k}\|_{X}\right)^{\eta}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\eta}}<\infty\bigg\}.

The space
\ell^{\gamma,\infty}(X)
and its norm are defined in the same manner with the usual modification. These spaces are themselves
(quasi-)
Banach spaces with (quasi-)norm
\|\cdot\|_{\ell^{\gamma,t}(X)}
. For more information on these spaces, the reader is refered to
[
Tri78
, Ch. 1.18.1]
.

Remark 5.27
.

For us, it will be crucial to exploit the identity
\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{s_{1},s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}=\left\|(\Delta_{n}(f))_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{\frac{s_{2}}{d},q}(L_{q}(\Omega_{T}))}
.

Definition 5.28
.

Let
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
be (quasi-)Banach spaces with corresponding (quasi-)norms
\|\cdot\|_{X_{i}}
,
i=1,2
. Then, the
K
-functional
of
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
is defined for
x\in X_{1}+X_{2}
as

\displaystyle K(x,\delta,X_{1},X_{2}):=\inf\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}\in X_{1},\ x_{2}\in X_{2}\\
x_{1}+x_{2}=x\end{subarray}}\|x_{1}\|_{X_{1}}+\delta\|x_{2}\|_{X_{2}},\quad\delta>0.

Using this functional, we can define the
(real) interpolation space of
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
for any
\theta\in(0,1)
and
\eta\in(0,\infty)
via

\displaystyle(X_{1},X_{2})_{(\theta,\eta)}:=\bigg\{x\in X_{1}+X_{2}\ \Big|\ \|x\|_{(X_{1},X_{2})_{(\theta,\eta)}}:=\Big(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\delta^{-\theta}K(x,\delta,X_{1},X_{2})\Big]^{\eta}\frac{d\delta}{\delta}\right)^{\frac{1}{\eta}}<\infty\bigg\}.

For the general theory regarding the interpolation of quasi-Banach spaces with respect to the
“
K
-method”
, we refer to
[
Tri78
, Ch. 1.1–1.3 and Ch. 1.7]
14
14

14

The theory and the proofs in this source are generally only given for the Banach space case. Nevertheless, in several remarks the author explains that the theory can be extended easily to the quasi-Banach space setting.
. For our further investigations, we only mention some important results related to these interpolation spaces.

Remark 5.29
.

(i)

For any
a\in(1,\infty)
, the discrete interpolation (quasi-)norm given by

\displaystyle\|x\|^{*}_{(X_{1},X_{2})_{(\theta,\eta)}}:=\left(\sum\limits_{k=-\infty}^{\infty}\left[a^{k\theta}K(x,a^{-k},X_{1},X_{2})\right]^{\eta}\right)^{\frac{1}{\eta}}

is equivalent to
\|\cdot\|_{(X_{1},X_{2})_{(\theta,\eta)}}
on
(X_{1},X_{2})_{(\theta,\eta)}
with equivalency constants depending only on
a
,
\theta
, and
\eta
. This can be shown according to part (a) of the theorem in
[
Tri78
, Ch. 1.7]
.

(ii)

Assume that
X_{2}
can be continuously embedded into
X_{1}
and
a>1
as above in (i), then

\displaystyle\|x\|^{*}_{(X_{1},X_{2})_{(\theta,\eta)}}\lesssim_{\,a,\theta,\eta}\|x\|^{**}_{(X_{1},X_{2})_{(\theta,\eta)}}:=\|x\|_{X_{1}}+\left(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left[a^{k\theta}K(x,a^{-k},X_{1},X_{2})\right]^{\eta}\right)^{\frac{1}{\eta}}\quad\text{for}\quad x\in X_{1}.

Proof.

Since,
X_{2}\subset X_{1}
,
X_{1}+X_{2}=X_{1}
holds true. Therefore, any
x\in X_{1}
can be decomposed into
x+0\in X_{1}+X_{2}
. This yields,
K(x,\delta,X_{1},X_{2})\leq\|x_{1}\|_{X_{1}}
for any
\delta\in[0,\infty)
. Accordingly,

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{k=-\infty}^{-1}\left[a^{k\theta}K(x,a^{-k},X_{1},X_{2})\right]^{\eta}\right)^{\frac{1}{\eta}}\leq\|x_{1}\|_{X_{1}}\left(\sum\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(a^{-\theta\eta}\right)^{k}\right)^{\frac{1}{\eta}}\sim_{\,a,\theta,\eta}\|x_{1}\|_{X_{1}},

since the last sum is geometric with
a^{-\theta\eta}<1
.
∎

(iii)

An important result that we will need below, is a result on the interpolation of generalized sequence spaces which can be found, for example, in
[
Pee76
, Thm. 4 of Ch. 5]
and
[
BL76
, Thm. 5.6.2]
15
15

15

Again, technically, in the second source, the result is only stated directly in the case where
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
are Banach spaces. But the proof also works in the quasi-Banach space setting.
. Let
Y_{1}
,
Y_{2}
be (quasi-)Banach spaces,
\gamma,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\eta,\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in(0,\infty]
, and
\theta\in(0,1)
with
\gamma=(1-\theta)\gamma_{1}+\theta\gamma_{2}
as well as
\frac{1}{\eta}=\frac{1-\theta}{\eta_{1}}+\frac{\theta}{\eta_{2}}
, then

\displaystyle\left(\ell^{\gamma_{1},\eta_{1}}(Y_{1}),\ell^{\gamma_{2},\eta_{2}}(Y_{2})\right)_{(\theta,\eta)}=\ell^{\gamma,\eta}\left((Y_{1},Y_{2})_{(\theta,\eta)}\right)

in the sense of equivalent (quasi-)norms. The corresponding constants depend on
\gamma_{i}
,
\eta_{i}
,
i=1,2
,
\theta
, and the (quasi-)triangle inequality constants of
Y_{i}
,
i=1,2
.

(iv)

A similar result holds true for Lebesgue spaces, as proven in
[
Tri78
, Ch. 1.18.4]
. Let
\eta,\eta_{1},\eta_{2},
and
\theta\in(0,1)
as above in
(iii)
. Then,

\displaystyle\left(L_{\eta_{0}}(\Omega_{T}),L_{\eta_{1}}(\Omega_{T})\right)_{(\theta,\eta)}=L_{\eta}(\Omega_{T})

holds true in the sense of equivalent (quasi-)norms with involved constants depending on
\eta_{1}
,
\eta_{2}
, and
\theta
.

These results lead to the following lemma that we will need at the end of the section when we prove the inverse estimate result.

Lemma 5.30
.

Let the parameters be chosen as in
5.29
(iii)
. Then the following equality holds in the sense of equivalent (quasi-)norms:

\displaystyle\left(\ell^{\gamma_{1},\eta_{1}}\left(L_{\eta_{1}}(\Omega_{T})\right),\ell^{\gamma_{2},\eta_{2}}\left(L_{\eta_{2}}(\Omega_{T})\right)\right)_{(\theta,\eta)}=\ell^{\gamma,\eta}\left(L_{\eta}(\Omega_{T})\right).

The equivalency constants depend on the parameters
\gamma_{i}
,
\eta_{i}
,
i=1,2
, and
\theta
.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of the iterative application of
5.29
(iii)
and
(iv)
.
∎

Further, we can relate the
K
-functional of a function
f\in\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})
with respect to the generalized anisotropic Besov spaces to that of its multiscale decomposition with respect to generalized sequence spaces. A corresponding result has been proven in
[
DP88
, Thm. 6.1]
.

Lemma 5.31
.

Let
5.21
hold. Choose
f\in\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})
and
\delta\in[0,\infty)
. Then,

\displaystyle K\left((\Delta_{n}(f))_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}},\delta,\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d},p}(L_{p}(\Omega_{T})),\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}{d},q}(L_{q}(\Omega_{T}))\right)\lesssim K\left(f,\delta,\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T}),\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})\right).

with a constant depending only on
d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
, and
\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
.

Proof.

Let
g\in\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})
, then
f-g\in\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})
according to
5.25
, and recall that
\pi_{n}(f)=\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}\left(\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\mathds{1}_{I\times S}\mathcal{B}_{\rho,I\times S}(f)\right)
, where
\mathcal{B}_{\rho,I\times S}(f)
is a best approximation of
f
in
\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}(I\times S)
with respect to
L_{\rho}(I\times S)
. According to
[
DP88
, Lem. 6.2]
, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}
, there are (quasi-)best approximations
\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{\rho,\mathcal{P}_{n}}(g)
of
g
in
\Pi^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{t,\bm{x}}(I\times S)
with respect to
L_{\rho}(I\times S)
such that
\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{\rho,\mathcal{P}_{n}}(f-g):=\mathcal{B}_{\rho,\mathcal{P}_{n}}(f)-\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{\rho,\mathcal{P}_{n}}(g)
is a (quasi-)best approximation of
f-g
. Further, the corresponding constants involved in the (quasi-)best approximation property only depend on the constant involved in the (quasi-)triangle inequality of the space
\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})
which only depends on
p
. Now, for
h\in\{g,f-g\}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, set

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}_{n}(h):=\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}\left(\sum\limits_{I\times S\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\mathds{1}_{I\times S}\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{\rho,I\times S}(h)\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{\Delta}_{n}(h):=\overline{\pi}_{n}(h)-\overline{\pi}_{n-1}(h)\quad\text{with}\quad\overline{\pi}_{-1}:=0.

Since we have chosen
\rho\leq q<p
, we can show

\displaystyle\left\|g-\overline{\pi}_{n}(g)\right\|_{L_{q}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}E(g,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}},\Omega_{T})_{q}\quad\text{and}\quad

\displaystyle\left\|(f-g)-\overline{\pi}_{n}(f-g)\right\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}E(f-g,\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}},\Omega_{T})_{p}

as in
4.22
. This allows us to establish

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\overline{\Delta}_{n}(g)\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}{d},q}(L_{q}(\Omega_{T}))}\sim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|g\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{\Delta}\quad\text{and}\quad

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\overline{\Delta}_{n}(f-g)\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d},p}(L_{p}(\Omega_{T}))}\sim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f-g\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}^{\Delta}

analogously to
5.10
. Now

\displaystyle K\Big((\Delta_{n}(f))_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}},\delta,\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d},p}(

\displaystyle L_{p}(\Omega_{T})),\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}{d},q}(L_{q}(\Omega_{T}))\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(\overline{\Delta}_{n}(f-g)\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d},p}(L_{p}(\Omega_{T}))}+\delta\left\|\left(\overline{\Delta}_{n}(g)\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}{d},q}(L_{q}(\Omega_{T}))}

\displaystyle\sim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f-g\|^{\Delta}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}+\delta\|g\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{\Delta}

due to
\left(\Delta_{n}(f)\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}=\left(\overline{\Delta}_{n}(f-g)\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}+\left(\overline{\Delta}_{n}(g)\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, which is a consequence of the linearity of
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}
. Taking the infimum over all
g\in\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})
now yields the assertion.
∎

Remark 5.32
.

Note, that we did not use the classical retraction-coretraction method as in
[
GM14
, Proof of Thm. 2.5]
. This is due to the fact that the method requires the operators to be linear, which might not be the case when
\rho\in(0,1)
. We circumvent this by the above lemma using a corresponding idea from
[
DP88
, Sect. 6]
.

Additionally, we can bound the
K
-functional of a function by a suitable sum of its
N
-term approximation errors using anisotropic finite elements.

Lemma 5.33
.

Let
5.21
hold,
f\in\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then we can estimate

\displaystyle K\bigg(f,2^{-\frac{n}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}},

\displaystyle\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T}),\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,\begin{subarray}{c}d,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\\
\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),\#\mathcal{P}_{0}\end{subarray}}2^{-\frac{n}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\left[\left(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}\left(2^{\frac{k}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\sigma_{2^{k}}(f)_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}\right)^{q^{*}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}+\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}\right],

where
\sigma_{N}(f)_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, is an abbreviated notation for the corresponding
N
-term approximation error defined in
Section

2.2
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\sigma_{N}(f)_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}:=\sigma_{N}(f,\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T}),\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\bullet},\textup{PATCH}\_\textup{REFINE}(\cdot,\cdot,d,\tilde{\alpha_{1}},\tilde{\alpha_{2}})).

Proof.

For given
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbb{P}_{N}
is finite and
\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
is finite dimensional, therefore
\sigma_{N}(f)_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}
is actually attained by some
\mathcal{P}\in\mathbb{P}_{N}
and
F\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\mathcal{P}}
. Let now
F_{k}\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{k}}
with
\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{k}\in\mathbb{P}_{2^{k}}
such that
\|f-F_{k}\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}}=\sigma_{2^{k}}(f)_{\widehat{B}_{p,p}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(\Omega_{T})}
for every
k\in\{0,\dots,n\}
. Additionally define
F_{-1}:=0
and
R_{k}:=F_{k}-F_{k-1}
for
k\in\{0,\dots,n\}
. Thus, since
F_{k}\in\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{k}}\subset\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})
and
\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})\hookrightarrow\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})
due to
5.25
, we can estimate

\displaystyle 2^{\frac{n}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}

\displaystyle K\bigg(f,2^{-\frac{n}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}},\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p},\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\frac{n}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\left(2^{-\frac{n}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\|F_{n}\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}+\|f-F_{n}\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}R_{k}\right\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}+2^{\frac{n}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\|f-F_{n}\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}\left\|R_{k}\right\|^{q^{*}}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}+2^{\frac{nq^{*}}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\|f-F_{n}\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}^{q^{*}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0}),\#\mathcal{P}_{0}}\left(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}2^{\frac{kq^{*}}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\left\|R_{k}\right\|^{q^{*}}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}+2^{\frac{nq^{*}}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\|f-F_{n}\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}^{q^{*}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}2^{\frac{kq^{*}}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\left(\left\|f-F_{k}\right\|^{q^{*}}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}+\left\|f-F_{k-1}\right\|^{q^{*}}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}\right)+2^{\frac{nq^{*}}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\|f-F_{n}\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}^{q^{*}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,s_{1},s_{2}}\left(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}\left(2^{\frac{k}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}\sigma_{2^{k}}(f)_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}\right)^{q^{*}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}+\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})},

where we have expanded
F_{n}
in a telescoping sum in the second, used the embedding
\ell^{q^{*}}(\mathbb{N}_{0})\hookrightarrow\ell^{1}(\mathbb{N}_{0})
and the subadditivity of
\|\cdot\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}^{q^{*}}
in the third step,
5.23
in the fourth, and the choice of
F_{k}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\cup\{-1\}
in the last step.
∎

Further, we need the following embedding result for generalized anisotropic Besov spaces with negative smoothness.

Lemma 5.34
.

Assume
\tau\in(0,\infty]
and let
5.21
hold true with
\alpha_{i}<0
,
i=1,2
. Then
L_{p}(\Omega_{T})\hookrightarrow\widehat{B}_{p,\tau}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(\Omega_{T})
continuously with an embedding constant only dependent on
d,\rho,p,\tau,s_{1},s_{2},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
, and
\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
.

Proof.

Due to
4.22
,
\|\Delta_{n}(f)\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}
holds true with a uniform bound independent of
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Thus, for
f\in L_{p}(\Omega_{T})
,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\widehat{B}_{p,\tau}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim_{\,d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\|f\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})}\left(\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(2^{\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}\tau}\right)^{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau}}\lesssim_{\,d,\tau,\alpha_{2}}\|f\|_{L_{p}(\Omega_{T})},

where we have exploited that the last sum converges since
\alpha_{2}<0
.
∎

Now we can finally prove the inverse estimates from
2
.

Theorem 5.35
.

Under
5.21
, the embedding

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}},q}\Big(\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T}),\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\bullet},\textup{PATCH}\_\textup{REFINE}(\cdot,\cdot,d,s_{1},s_{2})\Big)\hookrightarrow\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})

is continuous with an embedding constant depending only on
d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},\alpha_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
, and
\#\mathcal{P}_{0}
. If additionally
(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{+}(s_{1},s_{2})
with
s_{i}^{\prime}<s_{i}
,
i=1,2
, then also

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}},q}\Big(L_{p}(\Omega_{T}),\mathbb{V}^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{\bullet},\textup{PATCH}\_\textup{REFINE}(\cdot,\cdot,d,s_{1},s_{2})\Big)\hookrightarrow\widehat{B}^{s_{1}^{\prime},s_{2}^{\prime}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})

continuously with an embedding constant depending on
d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},s_{2}^{\prime},r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
, and
\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})
.

Proof.

Choose an arbitrary but fixed
C\in(1,\infty)
and set
\tilde{s}_{i}=Cs_{i}
,
i=1,2
. Further, let
\tilde{q}\in(0,\infty)
be such that
\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}=\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\tilde{s}_{1}}+\frac{d}{\tilde{s}_{2}}}
. Then,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{s_{2}}{\tilde{s}_{2}}\right)\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d}+\frac{s_{2}}{\tilde{s}_{2}}\,\frac{\tilde{s}_{2}+\alpha_{2}}{d}=\frac{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}{d}\quad\text{and}\quad\left(1-\frac{s_{2}}{\tilde{s}_{2}}\right)\frac{1}{p}+\frac{s_{2}}{\tilde{s}_{2}}\,\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}=\frac{1}{q},

which implies

\displaystyle\left(\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d},p}(L_{p}(\Omega_{T})),\ell^{\frac{\tilde{s}_{2}+\alpha_{2}}{d},\tilde{q}}(L_{\tilde{q}}(\Omega_{T}))\right)_{\frac{s_{2}}{\tilde{s}_{2}},q}=\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}{d},q}(L_{q}(\Omega_{T}))

according to
5.30
, with involved constants depending on
d,p,s_{1},s_{2}
, and
\alpha_{2}
. Therefore, we can calculate

\displaystyle\|f\|^{q}_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1}+s_{1},\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}_{q,q}(\Omega_{T})}=\left\|\left(\Delta_{n}(f)\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|^{q}_{\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}+s_{2}}{d},q}(L_{q}(\Omega_{T}))}\lesssim_{\,d,p,s_{1},s_{2},\alpha_{2}}\|f\|^{q}_{\left(\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d},p}(L_{p}(\Omega_{T})),\ell^{\frac{\tilde{s}_{2}+\alpha_{2}}{d},\tilde{q}}(L_{\tilde{q}}(\Omega_{T}))\right)_{\frac{s_{2}}{\tilde{s}_{2}},q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,s_{1},s_{2},\alpha_{2}}\left\|\left(\Delta_{n}(f)\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|^{q}_{\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d},p}(L_{p}(\Omega_{T}))}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\left[2^{\frac{n\,\frac{s_{2}}{\tilde{s}_{2}}}{\frac{1}{\tilde{s}_{1}}+\frac{d}{\tilde{s}_{2}}}}K\left(\left(\Delta_{n}(f)\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}},2^{-\frac{n}{\frac{1}{\tilde{s}_{1}}+\frac{d}{\tilde{s}_{2}}}},\ell^{\frac{\alpha_{2}}{d},p}(L_{p}(\Omega_{T})),\ell^{\frac{\tilde{s}_{2}+\alpha_{2}}{d},\tilde{q}}(L_{\tilde{q}}(\Omega_{T}))\right)\right]^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,\begin{subarray}{c}d,\rho,p,s_{1},s_{2},\alpha_{2},\\
r_{1},r_{2},\kappa_{\mathcal{P}_{0}},\mu(\mathcal{P}_{0})\end{subarray}}\quad\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}^{q}+\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\left[2^{\frac{n}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}}K\left(f,2^{-\frac{n}{\frac{1}{\tilde{s}_{1}}+\frac{d}{\tilde{s}_{2}}}},\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T}),\widehat{B}^{\tilde{s}_{1}+\alpha_{1},\tilde{s}_{2}+\alpha_{2}}_{\tilde{q},\tilde{q}}(\Omega_{T})\right)\right]^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\,d,p,s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2},\#\mathcal{P}_{0}}

\displaystyle\quad\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}^{q}+\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{s_{1}}+\frac{d}{s_{2}}}-\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\tilde{s}_{1}}+\frac{d}{\tilde{s}_{2}}}\right)q}\left[\left(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}\left(2^{\frac{k}{\frac{1}{\tilde{s}_{1}}+\frac{d}{\tilde{s}_{2}}}}\sigma_{2^{k}}(f)_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}\right)^{\tilde{q}^{*}}\right)^{\frac{q}{\tilde{q}^{*}}}+\|f\|_{\widehat{B}^{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}_{p,p}(\Omega_{T})}^{q}\right]

(41)

where we have used the discrete estimates for the interpolation space norm from
5.29
(i)
